<tr><td>↳反 sure</td><td>/ˈʃʊr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>确定的，可靠的</td><td>a sure sign<br>可靠的迹象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Falling leaves are a sure sign that autumn has arrived.  落叶是秋天已到的可靠迹象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unquestioning</td><td>/ʌnˈkwestʃənɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不加质疑的，绝对的</td><td>unquestioning obedience<br>不加质疑的服从</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime demanded unquestioning obedience from all its officials.  该政权要求所有官员不加质疑地服从。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>anachronism</b></td><td>/əˈnækrənɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不合时代的事物；时代错误</td><td>a glaring anachronism<br>明显的时代错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sundial in the spaceship struck viewers as a glaring anachronism.  飞船里的日晷让观众觉得是明显的时代错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fad</td><td>/fæd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>（短暂的）流行，时尚</td><td>a passing fad<br>一时的风尚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Standing desks turned out to be more than a passing fad.  站立式办公桌最终不只是一时的风尚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fashion</td><td>/ˈfæʃən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>时尚，流行；方式</td><td>go out of fashion<br>不再流行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wide silk ties went out of fashion decades ago.  宽丝绸领带几十年前就不流行了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vogue</td><td>/ˈvoʊɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>流行，时尚</td><td>in vogue<br>正流行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loose linen shirts are in vogue again this summer.  宽松亚麻衬衫今年夏天又流行起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>analogous</b></td><td>/əˈnæləɡəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相似的，可比较的</td><td>analogous to a machine<br>类似于一台机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The human eye is roughly analogous to a camera in several ways.  人眼在若干方面大致类似于照相机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 akin</td><td>/əˈkɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相似的，类似的</td><td>akin to fear<br>类似于恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The feeling was akin to fear but much quieter.  那种感觉类似恐惧，但安静得多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cognate</td><td>/ˈkɑːɡneɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>同源的，同类的</td><td>cognate languages<br>同源语言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 English and Dutch are cognate languages with many shared words.  英语和荷兰语是有许多共同词汇的同源语言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comparable</td><td>/ˈkɑːmpərəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可比较的，类似的</td><td>comparable results<br>可比的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two schools produced comparable results again this year.  两所学校今年又取得了可比的成绩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 connate</del></td><td>/ˈkɑːneɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天生的；同源的</td><td>a connate talent<br>天生的才能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sense of rhythm seemed connate rather than carefully trained.  她的节奏感似乎是天生的而非刻意训练出来的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corresponding</td><td>/ˌkɔːrəˈspɑːndɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相应的，对应的</td><td>the corresponding period<br>相应时期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales rose sharply over the corresponding period last year.  销售额比去年同期大幅上升。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 matching</td><td>/ˈmætʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相配的，相同的</td><td>matching curtains<br>相配的窗帘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She bought matching curtains and cushions for the small room.  她为那个小房间买了相配的窗帘和靠垫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 parallel</td><td>/ˈperəˌlel/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相似的，对应的；平行的</td><td>a parallel case<br>类似的案例</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Historians found a parallel case in a much earlier war.  历史学家在一场更早的战争中找到了类似的例子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resembling</td><td>/rɪˈzembəlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>类似的，像…的</td><td>a shape resembling a heart<br>形似心的形状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cloud had a shape resembling a running horse.  那朵云的形状像一匹奔马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 similar</td><td>/ˈsɪmələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相似的，类似的</td><td>similar in appearance<br>外观相似</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two species are similar in size but differ in color.  这两个物种大小相似，但颜色不同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suchlike</td><td>/ˈsʌtʃlaɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、代词</td><td>诸如此类的（东西）</td><td>pens, paper and suchlike<br>笔、纸之类的东西</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We packed pens, paper and suchlike for the long trip.  我们为长途旅行装了笔、纸之类的东西。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 different</td><td>/ˈdɪfərənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不同的，有差异的</td><td>completely different views<br>完全不同的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The twins have completely different tastes in music.  这对双胞胎在音乐口味上完全不同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disparate</td><td>/ˈdɪspərət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迥然不同的，全然不同的</td><td>disparate viewpoints<br>迥然不同的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee somehow reconciled disparate viewpoints into a single workable proposal.  委员会竟把迥然不同的观点整合成一份可行的提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dissimilar</td><td>/dɪˈsɪmələr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不同的，不相似的</td><td>wholly dissimilar styles<br>完全不同的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two novels treat love in dissimilar ways.  这两部小说以不同的方式处理爱情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diverse</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不同的，多样的</td><td>diverse backgrounds<br>各不相同的背景</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The class brings together students from diverse cultural backgrounds.  这个班汇集了来自不同文化背景的学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unlike</td><td>/ˌʌnˈlaɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>不同的；不像</td><td>unlike his brother<br>与他兄弟不同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unlike his brother, he never enjoyed noisy crowds.  与他兄弟不同，他从不喜欢喧闹的人群。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>annex</b></td><td>/əˈneks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>添加，合并</td><td>annex a territory<br>吞并一块领土</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empire moved quickly to annex the small territory to its north.  帝国迅速吞并了北面那块小小的领土。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adjoin</td><td>/əˈdʒɔɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>邻接，毗连</td><td>adjoin the garden<br>与花园相连</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their land adjoins the old churchyard on the north side.  他们的地在北侧与老教堂墓地相连。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affix</td><td>/əˈfɪks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贴上，附加</td><td>affix a stamp<br>贴上邮票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please affix a stamp to the corner of the envelope before mailing it.  寄出之前请在信封角上贴一张邮票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 append</td><td>/əˈpend/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>附加，增补</td><td>append a note<br>附上一条注释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She appended a short note to the end of the report.  她在报告末尾附上了一条简短注释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attach</td><td>/əˈtætʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>附上，粘上；使依附</td><td>attach a label<br>贴上标签</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please attach a label to each box before shipping it.  发货前请在每个箱子上贴标签。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 subjoin</del></td><td>/səbˈdʒɔɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>在末尾附加</td><td>subjoin a remark<br>在文末附加一句评语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor subjoined a brief comment to the printed letter.  编辑在刊印的信后附加了一段简短评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tack</td><td>/ˈtæk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>附加；用平头钉钉住</td><td>tack on a clause<br>附加一项条款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lawmakers tacked on a clause about rural schools.  议员们附加了一项关于乡村学校的条款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abate</td><td>/əˈbeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>减轻，减弱；减少</td><td>the storm gradually abated<br>风暴逐渐减弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fever finally abated after two days of medication.  服药两天后，烧终于退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deduct</td><td>/dɪˈdʌkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扣除，减去</td><td>deduct expenses<br>扣除费用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company deducts travel expenses from every invoice.  公司从每张发票中扣除差旅费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 remove</td><td>/rɪˈmuːv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>移除，去掉</td><td>remove all obstacles<br>清除所有障碍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers removed the fallen tree from the road quickly.  工人迅速将倒下的树从路上清除。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 subtract</td><td>/səbˈtrækt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减去，扣除</td><td>subtract the expenses<br>减去各项开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Subtract the shipping cost from the total price.  从总价中减去运费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>annotate</b></td><td>/ˈænəteɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给…作注解</td><td>annotate the manuscript<br>给手稿作注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scholars continue to annotate the manuscript with notes on its sources.  学者们仍在为这份手稿加上关于其来源的注解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commentate</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənteɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>现场解说，评述</td><td>commentate on a match<br>解说一场比赛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was hired to commentate on the evening matches.  他受雇解说晚间的比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 footnote</td><td>/ˈfʊtˌnoʊt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>脚注；加脚注</td><td>add a footnote<br>添加脚注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The translator added a footnote explaining the old proverb.  译者加了一条脚注解释那句古谚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gloss</td><td>/ɡlɑːs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>简短解释；敷衍潦草做事（甚至忽略）</td><td>gloss over the problem<br>敷衍带过问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor added a short gloss explaining the unfamiliar legal term.  编者加了一条简短的解释，说明这个陌生的法律术语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>antedate</b></del></td><td>/ˌæntiˈdeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>比…早，早于</td><td>antedate the invention<br>早于该项发明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These cave drawings antedate the invention of writing by many centuries.  这些洞穴壁画比文字的发明早了许多个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 antecede</del></td><td>/ˌæntɪˈsiːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>先于，在…之前</td><td>antecede the reform<br>先于那场改革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These local customs antecede the founding of the city itself.  这些当地习俗早于这座城市的建立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forego</td><td>/fɔːrˈɡoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>先于，在前；放弃</td><td>the foregoing remarks<br>前述的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The foregoing chapters describe that earlier period in detail.  前几章详细描述了那个更早的时期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predate</td><td>/priːˈdeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>早于，先于</td><td>predate the war<br>早于那场战争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These rock carvings predate the arrival of the first settlers.  这些岩刻早于最早定居者的到来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 preexist</del></td><td>/ˌpriːɪɡˈzɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>先于存在，早已存在</td><td>preexist the modern city<br>早于现代城市存在</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small farming villages preexisted the modern city by centuries.  小农村比现代城市早存在了数百年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 follow</td><td>/ˈfɑːloʊ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遵循，跟随</td><td>follow the rules<br>遵守规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 If you follow the instructions exactly, nothing can go wrong.  只要严格按说明操作，就不会出错。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 postdate</del></td><td>/ˌpoʊstˈdeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>晚于；填迟日期</td><td>postdate a check<br>把支票日期填迟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manuscript clearly postdates the fall of the empire.  这份手稿显然晚于帝国的覆灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 succeed</td><td>/səkˈsiːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>成功；接替</td><td>succeed in business<br>在生意上成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She succeeded in business where her brothers had failed.  她在兄弟们失败的生意上取得了成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>antipathy</b></td><td>/ænˈtɪpəθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感</td><td>mutual antipathy<br>相互反感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There has long been an antipathy between the two departments.  这两个部门之间长期存在反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animosity</td><td>/ˌænəˈmɑːsəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，憎恶</td><td>deep animosity between them<br>他们之间的深仇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of competition bred real animosity between the two families.  多年的竞争在两家之间催生了真正的敌意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animus</td><td>/ˈænɪməs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意</td><td>personal animus against him<br>对他的个人敌意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His criticism seemed driven by personal animus rather than honest doubt.  他的批评似乎出于个人敌意，而非真诚的质疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antagonism</td><td>/ænˈtæɡənɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>敌对，对抗</td><td>open antagonism toward reform<br>对改革的公开敌视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was open antagonism between the manager and his staff.  经理与员工之间存在公开的敌对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bitterness</td><td>/ˈbɪtərnəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>怨恨；苦味</td><td>lingering bitterness<br>挥之不去的怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old bitterness surfaced whenever the two brothers discussed money.  两兄弟一谈钱，旧怨就浮上来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enmity</td><td>/ˈenməti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，仇恨</td><td>long-standing enmity<br>积怨已久的仇恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old enmity between the families still poisons village life.  两家的旧仇至今仍毒害着村里的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gall</td><td>/ɡɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（使）焦躁，激怒</td><td>galled by the remark<br>被那句话惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug tone galled everyone sitting around the conference table.  他自鸣得意的语气激怒了围坐在会议桌旁的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grudge</td><td>/ɡrʌdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>怨恨，仇恨</td><td>hold a grudge<br>心怀怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She held a grudge against her cousin for more than ten years.  她对表姐怀恨在心十多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hostility</td><td>/hɑːˈstɪləti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，敌对</td><td>open hostility toward<br>对…公开的敌意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan met with open hostility from local residents.  该计划遭到当地居民的公开敌视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jaundice</td><td>/ˈdʒɔːndɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（因嫉妒或厌世而产生的）偏见</td><td>view with jaundice<br>带偏见看待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long unemployment had filled him with jaundice toward everyone still working.  长期失业使他对所有还有工作的人心生偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rancor</td><td>/ˈræŋkər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，深仇</td><td>speak without rancor<br>不带怨恨地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described the long dispute without any rancor toward her opponents.  她讲述这场长期争端时对对手毫无怨恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amity</td><td>/ˈæməti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>友好关系，亲善和睦</td><td>a treaty of amity<br>友好条约</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two nations signed a treaty of amity and commerce.  两国签署了友好通商条约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>antiquated</b></td><td>/ˈæntɪkweɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老的，过时的</td><td>an antiquated system<br>过时的系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office still runs on an antiquated system built in the eighties.  办公室仍在使用八十年代建成的过时系统。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antique</td><td>/ænˈtiːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>古老的，古董的；古董</td><td>an antique wooden chair<br>一把古董木椅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She collects antique clocks that were made in nineteenth-century France.  她收集在十九世纪法国制造的古董钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 archaic</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，久远的</td><td>archaic legal language<br>古旧的法律用语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract still uses archaic terms that nobody understands.  这份合同仍在使用无人理解的古旧术语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dated</td><td>/ˈdeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的</td><td>a dated hairstyle<br>过时的发型</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The furniture looked hopelessly dated beside the building's new glass walls.  在大楼新的玻璃幕墙旁，这些家具显得过时至极。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fossilized</td><td>/ˈfɑːsəˌlaɪzd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>僵化的，化石化的</td><td>fossilized attitudes<br>僵化的观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The department's fossilized rules discouraged every new hire.  部门僵化的规定让每个新人失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moribund</td><td>/ˈmɔːrɪbʌnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>垂死的，即将消亡的</td><td>a moribund industry<br>垂死的行业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap imports left the region's textile industry moribund within a single decade.  廉价进口品在十年内就让该地区的纺织业濒于消亡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moth-eaten</td><td>/ˈmɔːθ ˌiːtən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虫蛀的；陈旧过时的</td><td>a moth-eaten coat<br>被虫蛀的外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a moth-eaten sweater from his college days.  他穿着大学时代那件被虫蛀的毛衣来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obsolete</td><td>/ˌɑːbsəˈliːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，被淘汰的</td><td>become obsolete<br>变得过时</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Once a marvel, the machine became obsolete within five years of its release.  这台机器曾是奇迹，上市五年内就被淘汰了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outdated</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌdeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，陈旧的</td><td>outdated equipment<br>过时的设备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their outdated software could not handle the new files.  他们过时的软件无法处理新文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outmoded</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈmoʊdɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，废弃的</td><td>outmoded ideas<br>过时的观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory still relies on outmoded machinery from the nineteen sixties.  这家工厂仍在使用二十世纪六十年代的过时机器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 outworn</del></td><td>/ˌaʊtˈwɔrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧的，过时的</td><td>outworn traditions<br>过时的传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She quietly abandoned the outworn methods of her teachers.  她悄悄抛弃了老师们过时的方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prehistoric</td><td>/ˌpriːhɪˈstɔːrɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>史前的；极古老的</td><td>prehistoric cave paintings<br>史前洞穴壁画</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Archaeologists found prehistoric tools beside the dried riverbed.  考古学家在干涸的河床旁发现了史前工具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rusty</td><td>/ˈrʌsti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>声音生涩沙哑的</td><td>a rusty voice<br>生涩的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years of silence his voice sounded rusty and thin.  多年不语后，他的声音听起来生涩而单薄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contemporary</td><td>/kənˈtempəˌreri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>当代的；同时代的</td><td>contemporary art<br>当代艺术</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery mixes ancient sculpture with contemporary photography.  这家画廊将古代雕塑与当代摄影混合展出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 current</td><td>/ˈkɜːrənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>现在的，通行的；水流，电流</td><td>current practice<br>现行做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The current version of the software fixes several old bugs.  该软件的现行版本修复了若干旧漏洞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modern</td><td>/ˈmɑːdərn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>现代的，新式的</td><td>modern architecture<br>现代建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Modern phones can do far more than early computers could.  现代手机的功能远超早期的计算机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recent</td><td>/ˈriːsənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>最近的，近来的</td><td>in recent years<br>近几年来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Recent studies suggest that short naps improve memory.  最近的研究表明小睡能改善记忆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>antithetical</b></td><td>/ˌæntɪˈθetɪkl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>完全对立的，相反的</td><td>antithetical to freedom<br>与自由完全对立</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Censorship is antithetical to everything the university claims to defend.  审查制度与大学声称维护的一切完全对立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antipodal</td><td>/ænˈtɪpədəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正相反的，完全对立的</td><td>antipodal views<br>完全对立的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two economists hold antipodal views on taxation.  两位经济学家在税收问题上持完全对立的观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antipodean</td><td>/ænˌtɪpəˈdiːən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>截然相反的；对面的</td><td>antipodean opinions<br>截然相反的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their antipodean opinions made any agreement almost impossible.  他们截然相反的意见使达成一致几乎不可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contradictory</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈdɪktəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相互矛盾的</td><td>contradictory evidence<br>相互矛盾的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witnesses gave contradictory accounts of the accident.  证人们对事故给出了相互矛盾的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contrary</td><td>/kənˈtreri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>执拗的，故意作对的</td><td>a contrary child<br>执拗的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contrary child refused whatever his mother suggested.  这执拗的孩子对母亲的建议一概拒绝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diametric</td><td>/ˌdaɪəˈmetrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>截然相反的</td><td>in diametric opposition<br>完全对立</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two reports stand in diametric opposition to each other.  两份报告完全相互对立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 opposite</td><td>/ˈɑːpəzət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相反的；对面的</td><td>the opposite direction<br>相反的方向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two cars sped off in opposite directions.  两辆车朝相反方向疾驰而去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 polar</td><td>/ˈpoʊlər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>完全对立的；极地的</td><td>polar opposites<br>截然相反的两极</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brothers are polar opposites in almost every way.  兄弟俩几乎在各方面都截然相反。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 equivalent</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvələnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>等价的，相等的</td><td>the equivalent amount<br>等量的数额</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered her the equivalent amount in shares instead of cash.  他们提出用等量的股份代替现金付给她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 identical</td><td>/aɪˈdentɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>完全相同的，同一的</td><td>virtually identical results<br>几乎完全相同的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two samples produced identical readings on every machine.  两个样本在每台机器上都得出相同读数。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 same</td><td>/ˈseɪm/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相同的，同样的</td><td>exactly the same size<br>完全相同的尺寸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both machines run at the same speed under normal load.  两台机器在正常负载下以相同速度运行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>apparition</b></td><td>/ˌæpəˈrɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>鬼魂，幽灵</td><td>see an apparition<br>看见幽灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man swore he saw an apparition in the hallway.  老人发誓他在走廊里看到了一个幽灵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ghost</td><td>/ˈɡoʊst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>残迹，一丝痕迹</td><td>the ghost of a smile<br>一丝淡淡的笑意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only the ghost of the old inscription remained on the stone.  石上只剩下旧铭文的一点残痕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phantasm</td><td>/ˈfæntæzəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>幻象，幻影</td><td>a fleeting phantasm<br>转瞬即逝的幻象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In the fog the tower looked like a phantasm floating above water.  雾中那座塔像是漂浮在水面上的幻影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phantom</td><td>/ˈfæntəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>幻象，幻影；虚幻的</td><td>a phantom of the past<br>过往的幻影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He kept chasing the phantom of an easy fortune.  他一直追逐轻松发财的幻影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shade</td><td>/ˈʃeɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>鬼魂，幽魂；阴影</td><td>the shades of the dead<br>亡者的幽魂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old legends say the shades of drowned sailors haunt that cove.  古老传说称溺亡水手的幽魂出没于那个小湾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shadow</td><td>/ˈʃædoʊ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>偷偷尾随</td><td>shadow a suspect<br>偷偷尾随嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two detectives agreed to shadow the suspect through the crowded market.  两名侦探决定在拥挤的市场里偷偷尾随嫌疑人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 specter</td><td>/ˈspektər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>幽灵；可怕的景象</td><td>the specter of war<br>战争的阴影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The specter of another famine hung over the whole region.  又一场饥荒的阴影笼罩着整个地区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spirit</td><td>/ˈspɪrət/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，志气；精神</td><td>show real spirit<br>表现出真正的骨气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small team played with spirit until the final whistle.  这支小队斗志昂扬地打到终场哨响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spook</td><td>/ˈspuːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>鬼，幽灵；使惊吓</td><td>afraid of spooks<br>怕鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children insisted that a spook lived in the attic.  孩子们坚称阁楼里住着一个鬼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sprite</td><td>/ˈspraɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>精灵，小妖精</td><td>a water sprite<br>水精灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In the tale a water sprite guards the hidden spring.  故事里有个水精灵守护着隐秘的泉水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vision</td><td>/ˈvɪʒən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>幻象，梦幻；视力；远见</td><td>have a vision of<br>看到…的幻象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The saint claimed to have seen a vision of a burning city.  这位圣徒声称看到过一座燃烧城市的幻象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 visitant</td><td>/ˈvɪzɪtənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>幽灵；来访者</td><td>a ghostly visitant<br>幽灵般的来访者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described a pale visitant standing at the foot of her bed.  她描述有个苍白的幽灵站在她床脚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wraith</td><td>/reɪθ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>幽灵，鬼魂</td><td>a pale wraith<br>苍白的幽灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A pale wraith seemed to drift along the empty corridor.  一个苍白的幽灵似乎在空荡的走廊上飘动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 entity</td><td>/ˈentəti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>实体，独立存在物</td><td>a separate legal entity<br>独立的法律实体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The subsidiary is now a separate legal entity from its parent.  这家子公司现在是独立于母公司的法律实体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substance</td><td>/ˈsʌbstəns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>要旨，实质；物质</td><td>the substance of his speech<br>他讲话的要旨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The substance of her complaint was that nobody had asked her.  她抱怨的实质是没人问过她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>applicable</b></td><td>/ˈæplɪkəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用的，可行的；相关的，有关的</td><td>applicable to all students<br>适用于所有学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is applicable to all students regardless of their year.  这条规定适用于所有学生，不论他们是哪个年级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 actionable</td><td>/ˈækʃənəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可操作的；可起诉的</td><td>actionable advice<br>可操作的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The consultant offered actionable advice instead of vague theory.  顾问给出了可操作的建议而非空泛理论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 applicative</td><td>/ˈæplɪkeɪtɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适用的，应用性的</td><td>applicative research<br>应用性研究</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lab focuses on applicative research with clear industrial uses.  该实验室专注于有明确工业用途的应用性研究。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 applied</td><td>/əˈplaɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>应用的，实用的</td><td>applied mathematics<br>应用数学</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She switched from pure to applied mathematics in her third year.  她第三年从纯数学转到应用数学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 functional</td><td>/ˈfʌŋkʃənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>实用的，功能性的</td><td>functional furniture<br>实用的家具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was plain but completely functional.  厨房很朴素，但完全实用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 practicable</td><td>/ˈpræktəkəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可实行的，行得通的</td><td>practicable measures<br>可实行的措施</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repairs must begin as soon as it is practicable.  一旦条件允许就必须开始维修。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 serviceable</td><td>/ˈsɜːrvəsəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用的，耐用的</td><td>still serviceable<br>仍可使用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old tractor is battered but still perfectly serviceable.  这台旧拖拉机破旧，但仍完全可用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 useful</td><td>/ˈjuːsfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有用的，有益的</td><td>a useful tool<br>一件有用的工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This old dictionary is still useful for checking rare words.  这本旧词典查生僻词仍然很有用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 workable</td><td>/ˈwɜːrkəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可行的，行之有效的</td><td>a workable compromise<br>行得通的妥协</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They reached a workable arrangement about the shared yard.  他们就共用院子达成了可行的安排。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apposite</td><td>/ˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，合适的</td><td>an apposite comment<br>恰当的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made one apposite comment that settled the whole argument.  她一句恰当的评论就平息了整场争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apropos</td><td>/ˌæprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>相关的；关于，有关</td><td>apropos of nothing<br>毫无来由地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Apropos of nothing, he asked whether I had ever been to Rome.  他毫无来由地问我是否去过罗马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 germane</td><td>/dʒɜːrˈmeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关的，适当的</td><td>germane to the discussion<br>与讨论相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her question was germane to the discussion and changed our plan.  她的问题与讨论密切相关，改变了我们的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 material</td><td>/məˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>物质的；重要的；材料</td><td>material comforts<br>物质上的享受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cared little for material wealth and lived very simply.  他不太在意物质财富，生活极为简朴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pointed</td><td>/ˈpɔɪntɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖锐的，一针见血的</td><td>a pointed question<br>尖锐的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She asked a pointed question that the minister could not answer.  她提出了一个部长无法回答的尖锐问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relative</td><td>/ˈrelətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相关的；相对的；亲属</td><td>relative to the topic<br>与该话题相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Comfort is relative and depends largely on what you expect.  舒适是相对的，很大程度上取决于你的期望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relevant</td><td>/ˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关系的，重要的</td><td>relevant to the case<br>与本案有关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the old letters were relevant to the case.  法官裁定那些旧信件与本案有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impractical</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpræktəkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不切实际的</td><td>an impractical plan<br>不切实际的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building a bridge there proved impractical and hugely expensive.  在那里建桥被证明不切实际且极其昂贵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inapplicable</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈplɪkəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不适用的，不相关的</td><td>inapplicable to this case<br>不适用于本案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old rule is inapplicable to digital contracts.  旧规则不适用于电子合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unusable</td><td>/ʌnˈjuːzəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不能用的，无法使用的</td><td>rendered unusable<br>变得无法使用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Flooding left the whole ground floor unusable.  洪水使整个一楼无法使用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unworkable</td><td>/ʌnˈwɜːrkəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>行不通的，无法实施的</td><td>an unworkable rule<br>无法实施的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Staff called the new schedule completely unworkable.  员工称新排班表完全行不通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 useless</td><td>/ˈjuːsləs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无用的，无效的</td><td>practically useless<br>几乎毫无用处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without a charger the expensive phone was completely useless.  没有充电器，这部贵手机完全没用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraneous</td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immaterial</td><td>/ˌɪməˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>immaterial to the case<br>与本案无关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Whether he liked the plan is immaterial to the legal question here.  他是否喜欢这个计划与这里的法律问题无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impertinent</td><td>/ɪmˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相关的；无礼的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impertinent remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected that the question was impertinent to the charges.  律师反对说，这个问题与指控无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inapposite</del></td><td>/ɪnˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恰当的，不相关的</td><td>an inapposite comparison<br>不恰当的比较</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comparison to wartime rationing seems inapposite today.  把它比作战时配给在今天显得不恰当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irrelevant</td><td>/ˌɪˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>an irrelevant detail<br>无关的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected because the question was completely irrelevant here.  律师提出反对，因为这个问题在此完全无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pointless</td><td>/ˈpɔɪntləs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无意义的，徒劳的</td><td>a pointless argument<br>毫无意义的争论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arguing with him about politics is completely pointless and exhausting.  和他争论政治完全没有意义，而且很累。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>apprehensive</b></td><td>/ˌæprɪˈhensɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>知晓的；忧虑不安的</td><td>apprehensive of the danger<br>知晓其中的危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was fully apprehensive of the dangers before she signed anything.  在签任何文件之前，她已充分知晓其中的危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alive</td><td>/əˈlaɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活着的，存在的</td><td>keep the tradition alive<br>让传统延续下去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The language is still alive in a few remote mountain villages.  这门语言在少数偏远山村中仍然活着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aware</td><td>/əˈwer/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>知道的，意识到的</td><td>be aware of the danger<br>意识到危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was fully aware that the deadline had already passed.  她完全知道截止日期已经过了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cognizant</td><td>/ˈkɑːɡnɪzənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>知道的，意识到的</td><td>be cognizant of risks<br>意识到风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors must be cognizant of the risks before signing anything.  投资者在签署任何文件前必须意识到风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mindful</td><td>/ˈmaɪndfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，注意的</td><td>mindful of the risks<br>留意风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be mindful of the time when you answer each question.  回答每个问题时要留意时间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sensible</td><td>/ˈsensəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，合理的</td><td>a sensible choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing boots was a sensible choice in that mud.  在那种泥地里穿靴子是明智的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sentient</td><td>/ˈsenʃənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有知觉的，能感知的</td><td>sentient beings<br>有感知的生物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel asks whether machines could ever be sentient.  这部小说探讨机器是否可能有知觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ware</td><td>/ˈwer/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>警觉的，提防的；货品</td><td>ware of every danger<br>对每种危险都警觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old sailor was ware of every change in the wind.  这位老水手对风向的每一点变化都很警觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 witting</td><td>/ˈwɪtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>知情的，有意的</td><td>a witting accomplice<br>知情的共犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a witting participant in the scheme from the start.  她从一开始就是这个计划的知情参与者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 afraid</td><td>/əˈfreɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>害怕的，担心的</td><td>afraid of the dark<br>怕黑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The little boy is still afraid of the dark.  这个小男孩仍然怕黑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aghast</td><td>/əˈɡæst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>惊骇的，惊呆的</td><td>aghast at the news<br>对消息感到惊骇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stood aghast at the damage the storm had done.  她惊骇地看着风暴造成的破坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fearful</td><td>/ˈfɪrfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>害怕的，胆怯的</td><td>fearful of the dark<br>害怕黑暗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child was fearful of every shadow in the hallway.  这孩子害怕走廊里的每一个影子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frightened</td><td>/ˈfraɪtənd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>害怕的，受惊的</td><td>a frightened child<br>受惊的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frightened child clung to her mother's coat.  受惊的孩子紧抓着母亲的外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrified</td><td>/ˈhɔːrəˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感到恐惧的，惊骇的</td><td>horrified by the sight<br>被那景象骇住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors were horrified by what they saw inside the house.  邻居们被屋里看到的情形骇住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hysterical</td><td>/hɪˈsterɪkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>情绪失控的，歇斯底里的</td><td>become hysterical with grief<br>因悲伤而失控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child grew hysterical when she lost sight of her mother.  孩子看不见母亲时变得歇斯底里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scared</td><td>/ˈskerd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>害怕的，惊恐的</td><td>scared to death<br>吓得要死</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I was scared to death when all the lights went out.  灯全灭时我吓得要死。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shocked</td><td>/ˈʃɑːkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>震惊的</td><td>shocked into silence<br>震惊得说不出话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole audience sat shocked into complete silence.  全场观众震惊得完全沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terrified</td><td>/ˈterəˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极度惊恐的</td><td>terrified of heights<br>极度怕高</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is terrified of heights and refused to climb the tower.  他极度怕高，拒绝爬那座塔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignorant</td><td>/ˈɪɡnərənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知的，不知道的</td><td>ignorant of the facts<br>不了解事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They were completely ignorant of the danger ahead.  他们对前方的危险毫不知情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insensible</td><td>/ɪnˈsensəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知觉的，昏迷的</td><td>insensible to pain<br>感觉不到疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blow left him insensible for several minutes.  这一击使他几分钟内失去了知觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 oblivious</td><td>/əˈblɪviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不知道的，不知情的</td><td>oblivious to the noise<br>察觉不到噪音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Deep in her book, she was oblivious to the noise of the crowd.  她沉浸在书里，察觉不到人群的嘈杂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unaware</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈwer/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不知道的，未察觉的</td><td>be unaware of the rule<br>不知道这条规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They were unaware that the meeting had been moved to Friday.  他们不知道会议已改到周五。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unconscious</td><td>/ʌnˈkɑːnʃəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无意识的；昏迷的</td><td>an unconscious habit<br>无意识的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The driver lay unconscious on the road until help arrived.  司机躺在路上昏迷不醒，直到救援到来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unmindful</td><td>/ʌnˈmaɪndfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不留意的，未察觉的</td><td>unmindful of danger<br>不顾危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unmindful of the time, they talked until well past midnight.  他们没在意时间，一直聊到深夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwitting</td><td>/ʌnˈwɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不知道的，未觉察的</td><td>an unwitting accomplice<br>不知情的帮凶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became an unwitting participant in the whole scheme.  她不知不觉地成了整个阴谋的参与者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dauntless</td><td>/ˈdɔntləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无畏的，勇敢的</td><td>dauntless courage<br>无畏的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dauntless nurse worked through shelling to reach the wounded.  这位无畏的护士在炮击中赶去救伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fearless</td><td>/ˈfɪrləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无畏的，大胆的</td><td>a fearless reporter<br>无畏的记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was fearless in questioning the most powerful men present.  她敢于质问在场最有权势的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intrepid</td><td>/ɪnˈtrepɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的</td><td>an intrepid explorer<br>无畏的探险家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The intrepid explorer crossed the frozen strait alone in the middle of winter.  这位大胆的探险家在隆冬时节独自穿越了结冰的海峡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unafraid</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈfreɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不害怕的，无畏的</td><td>unafraid of criticism<br>不怕批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was unafraid of criticism and published the essay anyway.  她不怕批评，仍然发表了那篇文章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>arable</b></td><td>/ˈærəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适合耕种的</td><td>arable land<br>可耕种的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a narrow strip of arable land lies between the desert and the sea.  沙漠与海之间只有一条狭窄的可耕地带。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cultivable</td><td>/ˈkʌltɪvəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可耕种的</td><td>cultivable land<br>可耕地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a third of the island is cultivable land.  这座岛只有三分之一是可耕地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tillable</td><td>/ˈtɪləbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可耕的</td><td>tillable acres<br>可耕的田亩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The farm has forty tillable acres beside the river.  这个农场在河边有四十英亩可耕地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 barren</td><td>/ˈbærən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无成果的</td><td>barren land<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on the barren land beyond the last village.  最后一个村子之外的贫瘠土地上什么都长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sterile</td><td>/ˈsterəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无菌的</td><td>sterile medical instruments<br>无菌医疗器械</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Surgeons must use sterile instruments to prevent infection during any operation.  外科医生必须使用无菌器械，以防手术中感染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infertile</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrtəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；不育的</td><td>infertile ground<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drought left the ground almost completely infertile.  多年干旱使土地几乎完全贫瘠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfruitful</td><td>/ˌʌnˈfruːtfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不结果实的；无成果的</td><td>unfruitful soil<br>贫瘠的土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of unfruitful negotiation exhausted both sides.  多年无成果的谈判使双方疲惫不堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unproductive</td><td>/ˌʌnprəˈdʌktɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无成效的</td><td>unproductive rocky soil<br>贫瘠的石质土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drought left the fields dry and unproductive.  多年干旱使这些田地干枯贫瘠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>arbitrary</b></td><td>/ˈɑːrbətreri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横的，独断专行的；随意的</td><td>an arbitrary decision<br>武断的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rules seemed completely arbitrary to the students.  这些规定在学生看来完全是随意的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dictatorial</td><td>/ˌdɪktəˈtɔːriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>独裁的；专横的</td><td>a dictatorial manner<br>专横的作风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach's dictatorial manner made several players quit.  教练专横的态度让几名球员退出了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 willful</td><td>/ˈwɪlfl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的，倔强的</td><td>willful disobedience<br>故意的违抗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His willful refusal to ask for help delayed the repair by weeks.  他固执地拒绝求助，使维修推迟了好几周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aimless</td><td>/ˈeɪmləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无目的的，无方向的</td><td>an aimless life<br>漫无目的的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After graduation he drifted through several aimless years in the city.  毕业后他在城里虚度了几年漫无目的的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 desultory</td><td>/ˈdesəlˌtɔːri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>散漫的，无条理的</td><td>desultory conversation<br>散漫的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They made desultory notes instead of a real research plan.  他们做了些散漫的笔记，而非真正的研究计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 erratic</td><td>/ɪˈrætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反复无常的，不规律的</td><td>erratic driving behavior<br>飘忽不定的驾驶行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His erratic driving behavior alarmed the passengers long before the police stopped him.  早在警察拦下他之前，他飘忽不定的驾驶行为就让乘客惊慌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haphazard</td><td>/hæpˈhæzərd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>随意的；杂乱无章的</td><td>a haphazard manner<br>杂乱无章的方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The files were stacked in a haphazard pile on the office floor.  文件杂乱无章地堆在办公室的地板上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scattered</td><td>/ˈskætərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>分散的，零星的</td><td>scattered showers<br>零星阵雨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scattered books and papers covered every surface of his small desk.  零散的书和纸铺满了他小书桌的每一处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slapdash</td><td>/ˈslæpdæʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>马虎的</td><td>a slapdash job<br>马虎的活儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His report was so slapdash that the editor refused to publish it.  他的报告太马虎，编辑拒绝发表。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stray</td><td>/streɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫无目的的</td><td>stray thoughts<br>零散的念头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few stray thoughts kept interrupting her careful reading of the contract.  几个零散的念头不断打断她对合同的细读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 willy-nilly</td><td>/ˌwɪli ˈnɪli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>副词</td><td>杂乱无章地；不由自主地</td><td>scattered willy-nilly<br>杂乱地散落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Papers were scattered willy-nilly across the office floor.  文件杂乱地散落在办公室地板上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 methodical</td><td>/məˈθɑːdɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>井然有序的，有条理的</td><td>a methodical approach<br>有条理的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A methodical search of the archive finally produced the missing land records.  对档案的有条理搜查终于找出了缺失的土地记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orderly</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有秩序的，整齐的</td><td>an orderly queue<br>整齐的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students left the hall in an orderly line.  学生们排成整齐的队伍离开礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 organized</td><td>/ˈɔːrɡəˌnaɪzd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有条理的，有组织的</td><td>highly organized files<br>条理分明的文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She keeps an organized notebook for every course she takes.  她为每门课都备有一本条理分明的笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 regular</td><td>/ˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>规律的，通常的</td><td>on a regular basis<br>定期地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He goes swimming on a regular basis every week.  他每周都定期去游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 systematic</td><td>/ˌsɪstəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>系统的，有条理的</td><td>a systematic review<br>系统性回顾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team took a systematic approach to checking each component.  团队用系统的方法逐一检查了每个部件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 systematized</td><td>/ˈsɪstəmətaɪzd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>系统化的，有条理的</td><td>a systematized filing method<br>系统化的归档方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her notes were carefully systematized by subject and by date.  她的笔记按主题和日期作了系统整理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>arcane</b></td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难以理解的</td><td>arcane rules<br>晦涩难懂的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is full of arcane rules nobody understands.  税法充满无人理解的晦涩规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abstruse</td><td>/əbˈstruːs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>abstruse theories<br>深奥的理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lecture was too abstruse for first-year students.  他的讲座对一年级学生来说太晦涩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cryptic</td><td>/ˈkrɪptɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秘密的；难以理解的</td><td>a cryptic remark<br>费解的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His cryptic note gave us no hint about where he had gone.  他那张费解的便条没有透露他去了哪里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deep</td><td>/ˈdiːp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深刻的，深奥的</td><td>a deep analysis<br>深刻的分析</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The essay offers a deep reading of the novel's ending.  这篇文章对小说结尾作了深刻解读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enigmatic</td><td>/ˌenɪɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>an enigmatic smile<br>神秘的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enigmatic answer left the reporters guessing for several days.  她神秘的回答让记者们猜了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 esoteric</td><td>/ˌesəˈterɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥难懂的</td><td>esoteric knowledge<br>深奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She writes about esoteric topics in medieval music.  她写中世纪音乐中深奥的题目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hermetic</td><td>/hərˈmetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的；密封的</td><td>a hermetic style<br>晦涩的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet's hermetic style baffles most readers.  这位诗人晦涩的风格令多数读者困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impenetrable</td><td>/ɪmˈpenɪtrəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>impenetrable jargon<br>晦涩难懂的行话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract was written in impenetrable jargon that confused everyone.  合同用晦涩难懂的行话写成，让所有人都困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inscrutable</td><td>/ɪnˈskruːtəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高深莫测的，难以理解的</td><td>an inscrutable expression<br>高深莫测的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inscrutable smile gave nothing away about his plans.  他那高深莫测的笑容丝毫没有透露他的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recondite</td><td>/ˈrekəndaɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难解的</td><td>recondite arguments<br>深奥的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture grew so recondite that half the audience quietly left.  讲座变得如此深奥，一半听众悄悄离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncanny</td><td>/ʌnˈkæni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>离奇的，奇异的</td><td>an uncanny resemblance<br>异乎寻常的相似</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The twins share an uncanny ability to finish each other's sentences.  这对双胞胎有一种离奇的本事，能接上对方的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 easy</td><td>/ˈiːzi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易的，不费力的</td><td>an easy question<br>简单的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The homework looked easy, but it took him three hours to finish.  作业看起来容易，可他花了三个小时才做完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 facile</td><td>/ˈfæsl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易做到的；肤浅轻率的</td><td>a facile explanation<br>肤浅的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report offers a facile explanation for a very complex problem.  这份报告对一个非常复杂的问题给出了肤浅的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shallow</td><td>/ˈʃæloʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浅薄的，肤浅的；浅的</td><td>shallow water<br>浅水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arguments are shallow and easy to refute.  他的论点浅薄，很容易反驳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 simple</td><td>/ˈsɪmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单的，容易的</td><td>a simple task<br>简单的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Follow these simple steps and the machine will restart.  按这些简单步骤操作，机器就会重启。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 superficial</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈfɪʃl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表面的，肤浅的</td><td>a superficial understanding<br>肤浅的理解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has only a superficial knowledge of the subject.  她对这门学科只有肤浅的了解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>argument</b></td><td>/ˈɑːrɡjumənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（逻辑上的）论证，理由；争论</td><td>a convincing argument<br>有说服力的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He built a convincing argument for keeping the old bridge open.  他提出了一个令人信服的论证，主张保留旧桥通行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 altercation</td><td>/ˌɑːltərˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>激烈争吵，口角</td><td>a heated altercation<br>一场激烈的争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief altercation over parking escalated into a lawsuit.  一场关于停车的短暂争吵最后升级成了诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 controversy</td><td>/ˈkɑːntrəˌvɜːrsi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>争论，争议</td><td>spark a controversy<br>引发一场争议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting sparked a controversy that lasted for decades.  这幅画引发了持续数十年的争议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disagreement</td><td>/dɪsəˈɡriːmənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，不一致，争执</td><td>a sharp disagreement<br>激烈的分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long disagreement over funding stalled the whole project.  关于资金的长期分歧使整个项目停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dispute</td><td>/dɪˈspjuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>争论，争端；对…提出质疑</td><td>settle a dispute<br>解决争端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighbors are still in dispute over the fence.  邻居们仍在为那道围栏争执。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hassle</td><td>/ˈhæsəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>争吵；麻烦事</td><td>a hassle over money<br>为钱的一场争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They had a long hassle over who should pay.  他们为该谁付钱争吵了很久。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imbroglio</td><td>/ɪmˈbroʊlioʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，复杂的局面</td><td>a political imbroglio<br>政治上的乱局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister resigned in the middle of a diplomatic imbroglio.  部长在一场外交乱局之中辞职了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quarrel</td><td>/ˈkwɔːrəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>争吵，口角</td><td>pick a quarrel<br>找人吵架</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brothers had a bitter quarrel about their father's will.  兄弟俩为父亲的遗嘱激烈争吵了一场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squabble</td><td>/ˈskwɑːbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>为琐事争吵；口角</td><td>squabble over money<br>为钱争吵不休</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children squabble endlessly about who sits by the window.  孩子们为谁坐窗边没完没了地吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tiff</td><td>/tɪf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>小争吵</td><td>have a tiff with him<br>和他闹小别扭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a silly tiff with him about the dinner plans.  她和他为晚餐安排闹了一场无谓的小别扭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wrangle</td><td>/ˈræŋɡl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>纷争，争端</td><td>a legal wrangle<br>一场法律纷争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long legal wrangle over the land delayed construction for years.  一场关于这块土地的漫长法律纷争使工程推迟数年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 account</td><td>/əˈkaʊnt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>说明，解释；账户</td><td>give a full account<br>作出完整说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave a detailed account of what she had seen.  证人详细说明了她所看到的情况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accounting</td><td>/əˈkaʊntɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>说明，解释；会计</td><td>a clear accounting of costs<br>对成本的清楚说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board demanded a full accounting of the missing funds.  董事会要求对失踪资金作出完整说明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 argumentation</td><td>/ˌɑːrɡjəmenˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>论证，辩论</td><td>careful argumentation<br>严密的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The essay wins by careful argumentation rather than emotion.  这篇文章靠严密论证而非情绪取胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 case</td><td>/ˈkeɪs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>情况，案例；箱子</td><td>in that case<br>那样的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In every case we studied, the same pattern appeared again.  在我们研究的每个案例中，同样的模式都再次出现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 explanation</td><td>/ˌekspləˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>解释，说明</td><td>offer a plausible explanation<br>提出合理的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His explanation for the delay satisfied nobody at the table.  他对延误的解释没有让桌上任何人满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rationale</td><td>/ˌræʃəˈnæl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>基本原理，解释</td><td>the rationale behind the decision<br>决定背后的理由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody explained the rationale behind the decision to close the library.  没有人解释关闭图书馆这一决定背后的道理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reasoning</td><td>/ˈriːzənɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>推理，论证</td><td>sound reasoning<br>严密的推理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her reasoning was clear enough for the jury to follow.  她的推理清楚得陪审团都能跟上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>arid</b></td><td>/ˈærɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>枯燥无趣的；干燥的</td><td>an arid lecture<br>枯燥的讲座</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor's arid lecture emptied half the seats before the break.  这位教授枯燥乏味的讲座在课间前就空了一半座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 droughty</del></td><td>/ˈdraʊti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>干旱的，缺水的</td><td>a droughty summer<br>干旱的夏天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three droughty years ruined the wheat harvest.  三个干旱年头毁掉了小麦收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dry</td><td>/ˈdraɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>干的，干燥的；使干燥</td><td>bone dry soil<br>干透的土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hang the towels outside until they are completely dry.  把毛巾挂到外面直到完全干燥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sere</td><td>/sɪr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>干枯的，凋萎的</td><td>a sere brown field<br>枯黄的田地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 By August the meadow had turned sere and brown under the sun.  到八月，草地在阳光下变得干枯发黄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thirsty</td><td>/ˈθɜːrsti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>渴望的；口渴的</td><td>thirsty for knowledge<br>渴求知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young reporter was thirsty for a really big story.  这位年轻记者渴望一条真正的大新闻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waterless</td><td>/ˈwɔːtərləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无水的，干涸的</td><td>a waterless valley<br>无水的山谷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They crossed a waterless plain for two long days.  他们花了漫长的两天穿越无水的平原。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreary</td><td>/ˈdrɪri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调乏味的</td><td>a dreary afternoon<br>沉闷的下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a dreary afternoon indoors watching rain streak the windows.  我们在室内度过一个沉闷的下午，看着雨水划过窗玻璃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flat</td><td>/ˈflæt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调无光的，乏味的</td><td>a flat performance<br>平淡无味的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paint dried to a flat, lifeless gray.  油漆干成了一种无光而死板的灰色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 humdrum</td><td>/ˈhʌmdrʌm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调乏味的；平淡的</td><td>a humdrum routine<br>单调乏味的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left the humdrum job to travel and write full time.  她辞掉那份单调乏味的工作，去全职旅行和写作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 Jading</del></td><td>/ˈdʒeɪdɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌倦的，使人生厌</td><td>a jading routine<br>令人厌倦的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of jading paperwork dulled his early enthusiasm.  多年令人厌倦的文书工作磨掉了他早年的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jejune</td><td>/dʒɪˈdʒuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣乏味的</td><td>a jejune argument<br>乏味浅薄的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics dismissed the sequel as jejune and repetitive.  评论家认为这部续集乏味且重复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monochromatic</td><td>/ˌmɑːnəkroʊˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单色的</td><td>a monochromatic palette<br>单色的配色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The designer chose a monochromatic scheme of soft grays.  设计师选了柔灰的单色方案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monotonous</td><td>/məˈnɑːtənəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无变化的</td><td>a monotonous voice<br>单调的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His monotonous voice made the afternoon lecture almost impossible to sit through.  他单调的声音让下午的讲座几乎难以听完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pedestrian</td><td>/pəˈdestriən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平庸无奇的，令人厌倦的</td><td>a pedestrian style<br>平庸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reviewer called the plot pedestrian and the dialogue painfully flat.  评论者称其情节平庸，对话乏味得令人难受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stale</td><td>/steɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧乏味的</td><td>stale jokes<br>老掉牙的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stale jokes made the audience shift restlessly in their seats.  他老掉牙的笑话让观众在座位上不安地挪动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stodgy</td><td>/ˈstɑːdʒi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古板守旧的，乏味的</td><td>a stodgy old textbook<br>一本乏味的旧教材</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The course relied on a stodgy old textbook nobody enjoyed reading.  这门课用的是一本没人爱读的乏味旧教材。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tedious</td><td>/ˈtiːdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长乏味的</td><td>a tedious explanation<br>冗长乏味的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager gave a tedious explanation of the new filing system.  经理对新的归档系统作了冗长乏味的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wearisome</td><td>/ˈwɪrisəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌倦的，乏味的</td><td>a wearisome journey<br>令人疲乏的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wearisome journey took three days by slow train.  这段疲乏的旅程坐慢车要三天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 damp</td><td>/ˈdæmp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>抑制，使沮丧；潮湿的</td><td>damp their enthusiasm<br>打消他们的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain did not damp our enthusiasm for the trip.  下雨并没有打消我们对这次旅行的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dank</td><td>/dæŋk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>阴湿的</td><td>a dank cellar<br>阴湿的地窖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They stored the wine in a dank cellar beneath the kitchen.  他们把酒存放在厨房下面阴湿的地窖里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humid</td><td>/ˈhjuːmɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>潮湿的，湿润的</td><td>hot and humid weather<br>湿热的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The air was so humid that our clothes never dried.  空气如此潮湿，我们的衣服总也干不了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 moist</td><td>/ˈmɔɪst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微湿的，湿润的</td><td>keep the soil moist<br>保持土壤湿润</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cover the seeds and keep the soil moist for a week.  盖好种子并保持土壤湿润一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wet</td><td>/ˈwet/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>湿的，潮的；弄湿</td><td>soaking wet clothes<br>湿透的衣服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My shoes got wet crossing the flooded street.  我的鞋在穿过被淹的街道时湿了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absorbing</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrbɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，吸引人的</td><td>an absorbing novel<br>引人入胜的小说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found her account of the voyage utterly absorbing.  我觉得她对航行的叙述极其引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engrossing</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，令人专注的</td><td>an engrossing story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary was so engrossing that nobody left early.  这部纪录片如此引人入胜，没人提早离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gripping</td><td>/ˈɡrɪpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扣人心弦的，紧张的</td><td>a gripping thriller<br>扣人心弦的惊悚片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final chapter is gripping from start to finish.  最后一章从头到尾都扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interesting</td><td>/ˈɪntrəstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有趣的，引人入胜的</td><td>an interesting proposal<br>一个有趣的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an interesting objection nobody had anticipated.  她提出了一个无人预料到的有趣反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intriguing</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtriːɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人好奇的，有趣的</td><td>an intriguing question<br>引人好奇的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an intriguing point that nobody had considered.  她提出了一个无人考虑过的有趣观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 involving</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlvɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，扣人心弦的</td><td>an involving story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary is more involving than any thriller this year.  这部纪录片比今年任何惊悚片都更扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>array</b></td><td>/əˈreɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一批，一系列；排列</td><td>an impressive array<br>蔚为壮观的一批</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum displayed an impressive array of ancient coins.  博物馆展出了一批蔚为壮观的古钱币。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrangement</td><td>/əˈreɪndʒmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>排列，布置；安排</td><td>a neat arrangement<br>整齐的排列</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neat arrangement of tools showed real discipline.  工具整齐的排列显示出真正的条理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disposal</td><td>/dɪˈspoʊzəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>排列，部署；处理</td><td>the disposal of troops<br>兵力的部署</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general studied the disposal of troops along the ridge.  将军研究了沿山脊的兵力部署。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disposition</td><td>/ˌdɪspəˈzɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>倾向，性情</td><td>a disposition to argue<br>爱争辩的倾向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a natural disposition to question every rule.  她天生有质疑每条规则的倾向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distribution</td><td>/ˌdɪstrəˈbjuːʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>分布，分配</td><td>an even distribution<br>均匀的分布</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The map shows an even distribution of clinics across the county.  地图显示诊所在全县分布均匀。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ordering</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>排序，排列</td><td>the ordering of chapters<br>章节的排序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ordering of the chapters confused several reviewers.  章节的排序让几位评审感到困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sequence</td><td>/ˈsiːkwəns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顺序，序列；接续的事物</td><td>in the right sequence<br>按正确顺序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please perform the steps in the correct sequence.  请按正确顺序执行这些步骤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 setup</td><td>/ˈsetˌʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>布局，安排；装置</td><td>the studio setup<br>工作室的布置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their studio setup lets three people record at once.  他们工作室的布置让三个人可以同时录音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrange</td><td>/əˈreɪndʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>安排，筹划；整理</td><td>arrange the meeting<br>安排会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She arranged the whole trip in a single busy afternoon.  她在一个忙碌的下午就安排好了整趟行程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 classify</td><td>/ˈklæsəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>分类，归类</td><td>classify plants into families<br>把植物分成科</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Biologists classify these strange creatures as neither plants nor animals.  生物学家把这些奇怪的生物归为既非植物也非动物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 codify</td><td>/ˈkoʊdəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>编成条文，系统整理</td><td>codify the rules<br>把规则编成条文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee worked for years to codify these local customs.  委员会花了数年把这些当地习俗编成条文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dispose</td><td>/dɪˈspoʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>使倾向于；处理掉，丢弃</td><td>dispose of the waste<br>处理掉废弃物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Volunteers helped dispose of the waste left behind after the summer festival.  志愿者帮忙处理掉夏日节庆后留下的废弃物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 marshal</td><td>/ˈmɑːrʃəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>整理，集结；元帅</td><td>marshal the evidence<br>整理证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer marshaled the evidence into a clear sequence.  律师把证据整理成清晰的顺序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 organize</td><td>/ˈɔːrɡəˌnaɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>组织，筹划，安排</td><td>organize the conference<br>筹办会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three students organized the conference without any help from staff.  三名学生在没有职工帮助下筹办了这次会议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 range</td><td>/ˈreɪndʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>排列，整齐排开；范围</td><td>range the books neatly<br>把书整齐排好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He ranged the bottles along the shelf by size and color.  他把瓶子按大小和颜色沿着架子排好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 systematize</td><td>/ˈsɪstəmətaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使系统化，使条理化</td><td>systematize the records<br>使记录系统化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new director systematized the museum's messy records.  新馆长把博物馆混乱的记录系统化了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 draw up</td><td>/ˌdrɔː ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>停住；拟定</td><td>draw up a contract<br>拟定一份合同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A black car drew up outside the gate and waited.  一辆黑车在大门外停下并等着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lay out</td><td>/leɪ ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>规划，布置；详细说明</td><td>lay out a plan<br>拟定一份计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She laid out the whole plan in fewer than ten minutes.  她用不到十分钟就把整个计划说清楚了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disorder</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，无序；失调，疾病</td><td>public disorder<br>公共秩序混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was left in complete disorder after the party.  聚会之后房间被弄得一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disorganization</td><td>/dɪˌsɔːrɡənəˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，组织瓦解</td><td>widespread disorganization<br>普遍的混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden resignation caused months of disorganization in the office.  突然的辞职给办公室带来数月的混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>assail</b></td><td>/əˈseɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抨击，严厉批评</td><td>assail a policy<br>抨击某项政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Opponents assailed the plan as reckless and unfair.  反对者抨击该计划轻率且不公。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abuse</td><td>/əˈbjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>辱骂，抨击；不正当或不合理使用</td><td>abuse of power<br>滥用权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was accused of abusing his position for profit.  他被指控滥用职位谋利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bash</td><td>/bæʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抨击，严厉批评</td><td>bash the media<br>抨击媒体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Politicians love to bash the press at rallies.  政客们喜欢在集会上抨击新闻界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belabor</td><td>/bɪˈleɪbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>过分强调；痛打</td><td>belabor the point<br>反复纠缠一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is no need to belabor the point any further.  没有必要再对这一点过分强调下去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blast</td><td>/blæst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>爆炸；斥责，抨击</td><td>a bomb blast<br>炸弹爆炸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bomb blast shattered every window on the narrow street.  一次炸弹爆炸震碎了这条窄街上的每扇窗户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 castigate</td><td>/ˈkæstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈（公开）指责</td><td>castigate for negligence<br>因疏忽而受斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge castigated the company for its negligence.  法官严厉指责该公司的疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excoriate</td><td>/ɪkˈskɔːrieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；擦伤皮肤</td><td>excoriate the government<br>严厉批评政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics excoriated the film for its casual cruelty.  批评家严厉批评该片的随意残忍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lambaste</td><td>/læmˈbeɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉斥责，痛骂</td><td>lambaste the government<br>痛斥政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lambasted the film for its shallow, sentimental ending.  批评家严厉斥责这部电影结局浅薄而煽情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savage</td><td>/ˈsævədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>凶残的，野蛮的；猛烈攻击</td><td>a savage beating<br>野蛮的殴打</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics savaged the film for its careless treatment of history.  批评家猛烈批评该片对历史的草率处理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 scathe</del></td><td>/skeɪð/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；伤害</td><td>scathe with bitter sarcasm<br>用讽刺严厉批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers scathed the film for its lazy writing and dull acting.  评论者严厉批评这部电影剧本敷衍、表演乏味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slam</td><td>/ˈslæm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；猛击</td><td>slam the proposal<br>猛烈批评该提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics slammed the film for its careless plotting.  评论家因情节草率而猛烈批评这部电影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trash</td><td>/ˈtræʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>下层败类；垃圾</td><td>dismiss them as trash<br>视其为下层败类</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her letters show real contempt, calling the newcomers trash.  她的信里满是轻蔑，把新来者称作下层败类。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 vituperate</del></td><td>/vaɪˈtuːpəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谩骂，责骂</td><td>vituperate an opponent<br>谩骂对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist vituperated the mayor in every weekly article.  这位专栏作家在每周的文章里都痛骂市长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acclaim</td><td>/əˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>称赞</td><td>win critical acclaim<br>赢得评论界赞誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won wide critical acclaim across Europe and Asia.  她的第一部小说在欧洲和亚洲赢得了广泛的评论界赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commend</td><td>/kəˈmend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬，表扬</td><td>commend her courage<br>赞扬她的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The principal commended her courage in front of the whole school.  校长在全校面前赞扬了她的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compliment</td><td>/ˈkɑːmplɪmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞；恭维</td><td>pay someone a compliment<br>赞美某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paid her a warm compliment on her new painting.  他对她的新画作说了一句诚挚的赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hail</td><td>/ˈheɪl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冰雹；一阵（如子弹）</td><td>a hail of criticism<br>一阵猛烈的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soldiers advanced through a hail of bullets and smoke.  士兵们在弹雨与烟尘中前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>atrophy</b></td><td>/ˈætrəfi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>萎缩，衰退</td><td>muscles atrophy quickly<br>肌肉迅速萎缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Muscles atrophy quickly when a patient stays in bed for weeks.  病人卧床数周后，肌肉会迅速萎缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crumble</td><td>/ˈkrʌmbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>崩塌，瓦解；碎裂</td><td>crumble into ruin<br>崩塌成废墟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their alliance crumbled as soon as the money ran out.  钱一花完，他们的联盟就瓦解了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decay</td><td>/dɪˈkeɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>衰败，腐烂</td><td>fall into slow decay<br>逐渐衰败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The abandoned mill has been left to decay for decades.  这座废弃磨坊已任其衰败数十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decline</td><td>/dɪˈklaɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拒绝；下降，衰退</td><td>decline an invitation<br>谢绝邀请</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She politely declined the invitation to speak at the ceremony.  她礼貌地谢绝了在典礼上发言的邀请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degenerate</td><td>/dɪˈdʒenəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、不及物动词</td><td>衰退的，堕落的；恶化</td><td>degenerate into chaos<br>恶化为混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peaceful protest began to degenerate into shouting and shoving.  和平抗议开始恶化为叫骂和推挤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 descend</td><td>/dɪˈsend/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>下降；世代相传</td><td>descend from the summit<br>从山顶下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path descends steeply toward the river through thick pine forest.  小径穿过浓密的松林，向河边陡然下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devolve</td><td>/dɪˈvɑːlv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>退化，衰退；移交</td><td>devolve into chaos<br>退化为混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate quickly devolved into shouting and insults.  辩论很快退化为叫喊和辱骂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ebb</td><td>/ˈeb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>衰退，减弱；退潮</td><td>his strength ebbed away<br>他的体力渐渐衰退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public enthusiasm for the project ebbed over the winter.  公众对这个项目的热情在冬天渐渐消退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regress</td><td>/rɪˈɡres/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>退化，恶化</td><td>regress to childish behavior<br>退回到幼稚行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Under stress he tends to regress to childish and sulky behavior.  在压力下他往往退化为幼稚而怨怼的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retrograde</td><td>/ˈretrəɡreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>倒退的</td><td>a retrograde step<br>一次倒退之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Banning the new textbooks feels like a retrograde step for the whole district.  禁用新教材对整个学区而言像是一次倒退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
