<tr><td>↳近 rot</td><td>/ˈrɑːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>腐烂，腐坏</td><td>rot in the damp<br>在潮湿中腐烂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wooden posts had rotted through at the base.  木桩底部已经烂穿了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sink</td><td>/ˈsɪŋk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>下沉，下降；水槽</td><td>sink below the horizon<br>沉到地平线下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old boat began to sink soon after the storm.  暴风雨过后不久那条旧船开始下沉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 worsen</td><td>/ˈwɜːrsən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使变坏，恶化</td><td>worsen over time<br>随时间恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her cough worsened after a week outdoors in the cold.  在寒冷的户外待了一周后，她的咳嗽加重了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ameliorate</td><td>/əˈmiːliəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>改善，改进</td><td>ameliorate the situation<br>改善局面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new subsidy did little to ameliorate rural poverty.  新补贴对改善农村贫困几乎没有作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 improve</td><td>/ɪmˈpruːv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>改善，提高</td><td>improve the quality of<br>提高…的质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regular practice quickly improved her pronunciation and her confidence.  经常练习很快改善了她的发音和自信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 meliorate</del></td><td>/ˈmiliəˌreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>改善，使变好</td><td>meliorate living conditions<br>改善生活条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple drainage work meliorated conditions in the crowded district.  简单的排水工程改善了拥挤街区的状况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>attest</b></td><td>/əˈtest/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，为…作证</td><td>attest to his good character<br>证明他品行良好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several neighbors will attest to his good character in court.  几位邻居将在法庭上证明他品行良好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 argue</td><td>/ˈɑːrɡju/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>争论，争吵；主张</td><td>argue over money<br>为钱争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They argued over money for most of the evening.  他们为钱争吵了大半个晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 authenticate</td><td>/ɔˈθentəˌkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，鉴定为真</td><td>authenticate the painting<br>鉴定画作真伪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts were called in to authenticate the painting's signature.  专家被请来鉴定这幅画签名的真伪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 certify</td><td>/ˈsɜːrtəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证明，认证</td><td>certify the results as valid<br>证明结果有效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An independent lab must certify that the water is safe.  必须由独立实验室证明水质安全。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corroborate</td><td>/kəˈrɑːbəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用证据或权威证实；为…提供证据，支持</td><td>corroborate the witness's story<br>证实证人的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two neighbors corroborated the driver's account of the accident that night.  两位邻居证实了司机对那晚事故的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 substantiate</td><td>/səbˈstænʃieɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实</td><td>substantiate the claim<br>证实这一说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No evidence has emerged to substantiate his serious accusations.  没有证据能证实他严重的指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 support</td><td>/səˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持，支撑</td><td>support a proposal<br>支持一项提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most members voted to support the new proposal.  大多数成员投票支持这项新提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 testify</td><td>/ˈtestəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>作证，证明</td><td>testify in court<br>在法庭上作证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two neighbors agreed to testify about the noise.  两位邻居同意就噪音一事作证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 validate</td><td>/ˈvælədeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，确认；使有效</td><td>validate the results<br>验证这些结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Independent labs validated the results within three months.  独立实验室在三个月内验证了这些结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 verify</td><td>/ˈverɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，核实</td><td>verify the data<br>核实这些数据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two independent labs will verify the results before publication.  两家独立实验室将在发表前核实这些结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vindicate</td><td>/ˈvɪndɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>为…平反，为…辩护，使无罪；证明，证实</td><td>vindicate one's reputation<br>为自己的名誉洗冤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The results vindicated her unusual approach.  结果证明了她那不寻常的方法是对的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contradict</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈdɪkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，反驳；与…相矛盾</td><td>contradict the evidence<br>与证据相矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness's second statement flatly contradicted everything she had said earlier.  证人的第二份陈述与她此前所说完全矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disprove</td><td>/ˌdɪsˈpruːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>反驳，证明为假</td><td>disprove the theory<br>驳倒这一理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Magellan's voyage finally disproved the belief in a flat earth.  麦哲伦的航行最终驳倒了地球是平的这一信念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gainsay</td><td>/ˌɡeɪnˈseɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认</td><td>gainsay the evidence<br>否认证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one could gainsay the evidence the inspector had collected.  没有人能否认检查员收集到的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rebut</td><td>/rɪˈbʌt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>反驳，驳斥</td><td>rebut the accusation<br>反驳这项指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She rebutted every charge with documents and dated photographs.  她用文件和标注日期的照片反驳了每项指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 refute</td><td>/rɪˈfjuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驳倒，反驳</td><td>refute the claim<br>驳斥这一说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scientist used new data to refute the claim about rising temperatures.  这位科学家用新数据反驳了关于气温上升的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>attune</b></td><td>/əˈtuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使协调，使和谐</td><td>attune oneself to<br>使自己适应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers are attuned to the moods of their students.  好老师能敏锐感知学生的情绪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accommodate</td><td>/əˈkɑːmədeɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使适应；容纳；迁就</td><td>accommodate different needs<br>满足不同需求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The schedule was changed to accommodate everyone's travel plans.  日程被改动以适应每个人的出行计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conciliate</td><td>/kənˈsɪlieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；调解</td><td>conciliate angry customers<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager tried to conciliate the angry customers with refunds.  经理试图用退款来安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conform</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>使协调；遵照，遵从</td><td>conform to the standard<br>符合标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All new buildings must conform to strict safety codes.  所有新建筑都必须符合严格的安全规范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coordinate</td><td>/koʊˈɔːrdɪneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>协调，使配合</td><td>coordinate the relief effort<br>协调救援工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two agencies coordinated the rescue effort after the flood.  洪水过后两个机构协调了救援工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 key</td><td>/ˈkiː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>关键的；钥匙</td><td>a key factor<br>关键因素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trust is the key element in any long working relationship.  信任是任何长期工作关系中的关键要素。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reconcile</td><td>/ˈrekənsaɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使和解，协调</td><td>reconcile conflicting views<br>调和相互冲突的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is hard to reconcile these two versions of the story.  很难把这个故事的两种说法协调一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disorder</td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，无序；失调，疾病</td><td>public disorder<br>公共秩序混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was left in complete disorder after the party.  聚会之后房间被弄得一片混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 disorganize</del></td><td>/dɪˈsɔːrɡəˌnaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使混乱，扰乱</td><td>disorganize the schedule<br>打乱日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The storm disorganized every flight leaving the airport that day.  风暴打乱了那天所有离港航班。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disrupt</td><td>/dɪsˈrʌpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扰乱，打乱</td><td>disrupt the meeting<br>扰乱会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Protesters disrupted the ceremony by shouting from the balcony.  抗议者在楼上喊叫，扰乱了典礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>august</b></td><td>/ɔːˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>威严的，庄重的</td><td>an august assembly<br>庄严的集会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The august assembly of scholars rose when the old president entered.  当老校长进场时，这个威严的学者集会全体起立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguished</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卓越的，杰出的；高贵的</td><td>a distinguished scholar<br>杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prize went to a distinguished historian of medieval law.  奖项授予一位杰出的中世纪法律史学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imposing</td><td>/ɪmˈpoʊzɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏伟壮丽的</td><td>an imposing building<br>宏伟的建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An imposing stone building dominates the square at the city center.  一座气势宏伟的石砌建筑主宰着市中心的广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 portly</td><td>/ˈpɔːrtli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肥胖的；（旧）魁伟的</td><td>a portly gentleman<br>肥胖的绅士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A portly gentleman in a grey suit greeted us warmly.  一位穿灰西装的胖绅士热情迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 solemn</td><td>/ˈsɑːləm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>庄严的，郑重的</td><td>a solemn promise<br>郑重的承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceremony was solemn and lasted almost two hours.  仪式庄严肃穆，持续了近两个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 staid</td><td>/ˈsteɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拘谨的，古板严肃的</td><td>a staid old bank<br>古板的老银行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The staid law firm finally allowed casual clothing.  这家古板的律所终于允许穿便装。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stately</td><td>/ˈsteɪtli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>庄严的，堂皇的</td><td>a stately mansion<br>堂皇的宅邸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A row of stately elms lined the road to the house.  一排庄严的榆树沿着通往房子的路排开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flighty</td><td>/ˈflaɪti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的，反复无常的</td><td>a flighty young man<br>轻浮的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seemed flighty at first but proved remarkably reliable.  她起初显得轻浮，但事实证明非常可靠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 frivolous</td><td>/ˈfrɪvələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的；无关紧要的</td><td>a frivolous remark<br>轻率无谓的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge dismissed the frivolous lawsuit within a single morning.  法官在一个上午之内就驳回了这起无理的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 giddy</td><td>/ˈɡɪdi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻率不严肃的</td><td>a giddy schoolgirl<br>轻率的女学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The giddy schoolgirls laughed through the entire serious lecture.  那些轻率的女学生在整场严肃的讲座中笑个不停。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 goofy</td><td>/ˈɡuːfi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傻气的，滑稽的</td><td>a goofy grin<br>傻气的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered with a goofy grin and a shrug.  他傻笑一下，耸了耸肩作答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 silly</td><td>/ˈsɪli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a silly mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was silly to climb that tall ladder without help.  没人帮忙就爬那么高的梯子太傻了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undignified</td><td>/ʌnˈdɪɡnɪfaɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不庄重的，失体面的</td><td>an undignified scramble<br>不体面的争抢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ministers made an undignified rush for the exit.  部长们不体面地冲向出口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>auspicious</b></td><td>/ɔːˈspɪʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好兆头的</td><td>an auspicious beginning<br>吉利的开端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rain on the wedding day was considered an auspicious beginning.  婚礼当天下雨被认为是个好兆头的开端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bright</td><td>/ˈbraɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快活的，开朗的；明亮的</td><td>a bright smile<br>开朗的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted every customer with a bright and genuine smile.  她用开朗真诚的笑容迎接每位顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 encouraging</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人鼓舞的</td><td>encouraging early results<br>令人鼓舞的初步结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The first trial produced encouraging results for the new treatment.  首轮试验为这种新疗法带来了令人鼓舞的结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 golden</td><td>/ˈɡoʊldən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吉祥的，极好的；金色的</td><td>a golden opportunity<br>绝佳的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This is a golden opportunity that we should not waste.  这是我们不该浪费的绝佳机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heartening</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtənɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人振奋的</td><td>a heartening response<br>令人振奋的反响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was heartening to see strangers helping clear the flooded street.  看到陌生人一起清理被淹的街道令人振奋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hopeful</td><td>/ˈhoʊpfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有希望的，抱有希望的</td><td>hopeful signs<br>有希望的迹象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There are hopeful signs that the talks will finally succeed.  有令人抱有希望的迹象表明会谈终将成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 promising</td><td>/ˈprɑːməsɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有前途的，有希望的</td><td>a promising young writer<br>有前途的青年作家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is the most promising violinist the school has ever trained.  她是这所学校培养过的最有前途的小提琴手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 propitious</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吉祥的</td><td>a propitious moment<br>吉利的时机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They waited for a propitious moment to announce the merger.  他们等待一个吉利的时机来宣布合并。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 baleful</td><td>/ˈbeɪlfl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的</td><td>a baleful influence<br>有害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His baleful influence on the young players worried the coach.  他对年轻球员的有害影响让教练担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dark</td><td>/ˈdɑːrk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>阴郁的；黑暗的</td><td>a dark mood<br>阴郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fell into a dark mood after reading the letter.  读了那封信后他陷入阴郁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 direful</del></td><td>/ˈdaɪrfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，凄惨的</td><td>direful warnings<br>可怕的警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old prophet issued direful warnings about the coming winter.  老先知对将至的冬天发出可怕的警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doomy</td><td>/ˈduːmi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶兆的，阴郁悲观的</td><td>a doomy prediction<br>不祥的预言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Newspapers printed doomy forecasts about the country's economy.  报纸刊出了对国家经济的悲观预测。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foreboding</td><td>/fɔːrˈboʊdɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不祥的；不祥的预感</td><td>a sense of foreboding<br>不祥的预感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt a strange foreboding as the sky darkened.  天色转暗时她有种奇怪的不祥预感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gloomy</td><td>/ˈɡluːmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的</td><td>a gloomy mood<br>忧郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew gloomy whenever the winter rain lasted more than a week.  每当冬雨连下一周以上，她就变得忧郁起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 menacing</td><td>/ˈmenəsɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>威胁的，凶险的</td><td>a menacing look<br>凶险的眼神</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two menacing dogs stood at the end of the narrow lane.  两条凶恶的狗站在窄巷尽头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 minatory</del></td><td>/ˈmɪnətɔːri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>带来威胁的，有凶兆的</td><td>a minatory tone<br>带威胁的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sent a minatory letter demanding payment within one week.  他寄来一封带威胁的信，要求一周内付款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ominous</td><td>/ˈɑːmɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶兆的</td><td>an ominous silence<br>不祥的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An ominous silence fell over the crowd as the announcement began.  宣布开始时，人群陷入不祥的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 portentous</td><td>/pɔːrˈtentəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>预兆性的，凶兆的</td><td>a portentous silence<br>预示不祥的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A portentous silence fell over the room before the verdict was read.  宣读判决之前，房间里陷入一阵预示不祥的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sinister</td><td>/ˈsɪnɪstər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>阴险的，不祥的</td><td>a sinister smile<br>阴险的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was something sinister about the empty old house.  那座空荡的老房子有种阴森之感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 threatening</td><td>/ˈθretənɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>威胁的；（天气）要变坏的</td><td>a threatening gesture<br>威胁性的手势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made a threatening gesture and then walked quickly away.  他做了个威胁的手势，然后快步走开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>austere</b></td><td>/ɔːˈstɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，朴实无华的</td><td>an austere room<br>简朴的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He slept in an austere room with a bed and one chair.  他睡在一间只有一张床和一把椅子的朴素房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spartan</td><td>/ˈspɑːrtn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简朴的，节约的</td><td>spartan living conditions<br>简朴的生活条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks lived in spartan rooms with only a bed and a lamp.  僧侣们住在只有一床一灯的简朴房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stark</td><td>/ˈstɑːrk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严酷的，荒凉的；鲜明的</td><td>a stark contrast<br>鲜明的对比</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph shows the stark landscape of the desert.  这张照片展现了沙漠荒凉的景观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unadorned</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈdɔːrnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，无装饰的</td><td>unadorned walls<br>朴素的墙面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chapel was unadorned except for one small wooden cross.  礼拜堂除一个小木十字外别无装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deluxe</td><td>/dəˈlʌks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>豪华的，高级的</td><td>a deluxe suite<br>豪华套房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They booked a deluxe suite overlooking the harbor.  他们订了一间可俯瞰港口的豪华套房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lavish</td><td>/ˈlævɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>奢侈的，大量的，无节制的；挥霍，浪费</td><td>a lavish banquet<br>奢华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They hosted a lavish banquet for three hundred guests in the garden.  他们在花园里为三百位客人举办了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 luxurious</td><td>/lʌɡˈʒʊriəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奢侈的</td><td>a luxurious hotel suite<br>豪华的酒店套房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They spent their anniversary in a luxurious hotel suite overlooking the harbor.  他们在一间俯瞰港口的豪华酒店套房里度过了结婚纪念日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plush</td><td>/plʌʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奢华的，豪华的</td><td>a plush hotel suite<br>豪华的酒店套房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They spent their anniversary in a plush hotel suite overlooking the sea.  他们在一间俯瞰大海的豪华酒店套房里度过结婚纪念日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sumptuous</td><td>/ˈsʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>豪华的，奢侈的</td><td>a sumptuous banquet<br>一场豪华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel served a sumptuous banquet for the visiting delegation.  酒店为来访代表团摆了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>automatic</b></td><td>/ˌɔːtəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>机械的，不经思索的；自动的</td><td>an automatic apology<br>机械的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He murmured an automatic apology and hurried past me.  他机械地含糊道了个歉，匆匆从我身边走过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impulsive</td><td>/ɪmˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冲动的，鲁莽的</td><td>an impulsive decision<br>冲动的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying that car was an impulsive act he later regretted.  买那辆车是他后来后悔的冲动之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 instinctive</td><td>/ɪnˈstɪŋktɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>本能的，直觉的</td><td>an instinctive reaction<br>本能的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her instinctive distrust of the offer proved entirely correct.  她对这份提议本能的不信任完全正确。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 involuntary</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlənteri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非自愿的；无意识的</td><td>an involuntary reaction<br>不自觉的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Blinking is an involuntary response to sudden light.  眨眼是对突然强光的不自觉反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mechanic</td><td>/məˈkænɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>机械的，呆板的；机修工</td><td>a mechanic reply<br>机械的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His answers were mechanic, as if learned by heart.  他的回答很机械，像是死记硬背下来的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mechanical</td><td>/məˈkænɪkəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>机械的；敷衍呆板的</td><td>a mechanical response<br>机械敷衍的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a mechanical nod without listening to a single word.  他机械地点了点头，一个字也没听。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 natural</td><td>/ˈnætʃərəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自然的，正常的</td><td>a natural reaction<br>自然的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is natural to feel nervous before a big exam.  大考前紧张是很自然的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spontaneous</td><td>/spɑːnˈteɪniəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自发的，不经思索的</td><td>a spontaneous burst of applause<br>自发的一阵掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A spontaneous burst of applause followed the young pianist's final note.  年轻钢琴家弹出最后一个音，掌声自发响起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calculated</td><td>/ˈkælkjuleɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过深思熟虑的，有意的</td><td>a calculated risk<br>经过权衡的风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investing now was a calculated risk that finally paid off.  现在投资是经过盘算的风险，最终有了回报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deliberate</td><td>/dɪˈlɪbərət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的，故意的</td><td>a deliberate attempt<br>故意的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delay was deliberate, designed to weaken our position.  这次延误是故意的，目的是削弱我们的立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intentional</td><td>/ɪnˈtenʃənəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>故意的，有意的</td><td>an intentional foul<br>故意犯规</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The omission was intentional rather than a simple oversight.  这一遗漏是故意的，而非单纯的疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 planned</td><td>/ˈplænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>计划好的，有计划的</td><td>a carefully planned trip<br>精心策划的旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole ceremony was planned down to the last detail.  整个典礼被计划到了最后一个细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 predetermined</td><td>/ˌpriːdiˈtɜːrmɪnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>预先确定的，事先决定的</td><td>a predetermined outcome<br>预先确定的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics said the vote had a predetermined outcome.  批评者说这次投票的结果早已预定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 premeditated</td><td>/priˈmedəˌteɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>预谋的，事先策划的</td><td>premeditated murder<br>预谋杀人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Prosecutors argued that the robbery was carefully premeditated.  检察官认为这起抢劫是精心预谋的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 studied</td><td>/ˈstʌdid/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的；刻意的</td><td>studied indifference<br>刻意装出的冷淡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered with studied calm that impressed the whole panel.  他以刻意保持的镇定作答，让整个评审组印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>avowal</b></td><td>/əˈvaʊəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>承认，公开宣布</td><td>a public avowal<br>公开表明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His public avowal of support surprised everyone at the meeting.  他公开表明支持，令会上所有人意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affirmation</td><td>/ˌæfərˈmeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>肯定，断言</td><td>a clear affirmation<br>明确的肯定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vote was a clear affirmation of the new charter.  这次投票是对新章程的明确肯定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assertion</td><td>/əˈsɜːrʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>断言，声明</td><td>a bold assertion<br>大胆的断言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bold assertion was never supported by any real data.  他大胆的断言从未有真实数据支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 asseveration</del></td><td>/əˌsevəˈreɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>郑重声明，断言</td><td>a solemn asseveration<br>郑重的声明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His solemn asseveration of innocence clearly moved the jury.  他郑重声明自己无罪，明显感动了陪审团。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 avouchment</del></td><td>/əˈvaʊtʃmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>断言，郑重保证</td><td>a firm avouchment<br>坚定的保证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness offered a firm avouchment of the man's alibi.  证人坚定地为那人的不在场证明作保。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 claim</td><td>/ˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>声称，自称</td><td>claim to be innocent<br>自称无辜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He claims to have finished the whole course in one weekend.  他自称一个周末就上完了整门课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 declaration</td><td>/ˌdekləˈreɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>声明，宣告</td><td>a public declaration<br>公开声明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company issued a public declaration of its new policy.  公司发布了新政策的公开声明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insistence</td><td>/ɪnˈsɪstəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>坚持，坚决主张</td><td>at his insistence<br>在他的坚持下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At his insistence the meeting was moved to Monday morning.  在他的坚持下会议改到了周一上午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profession</td><td>/prəˈfeʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专业，职业</td><td>the medical profession<br>医疗行业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Law was the only profession his family respected.  法律是他家中唯一看重的职业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disavowal</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈvaʊəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>否认，拒绝承认</td><td>a public disavowal<br>公开的否认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party issued a swift disavowal of the leaked memo.  该党迅速公开否认了泄露的备忘录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>backfire</b></td><td>/ˌbækˈfaɪər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>产生相反的结果，事与愿违</td><td>the plan backfired<br>计划事与愿违</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their attempt to silence the press was bound to backfire.  他们压制新闻界的做法必然事与愿违。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boomerang</td><td>/ˈbuːməˌræŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>事与愿违，反伤自身；回旋镖</td><td>boomerang on him<br>反过来伤到他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smear campaign boomeranged on its own organizers.  抹黑运动反过来伤了组织者自己。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 succeed</td><td>/səkˈsiːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>成功；接替</td><td>succeed in business<br>在生意上成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She succeeded in business where her brothers had failed.  她在兄弟们失败的生意上取得了成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>backslide</b></td><td>/ˈbækslaɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（情况等）倒退，变坏</td><td>backslide into old habits<br>又退回旧习</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many patients backslide into old habits after leaving the clinic.  许多病人离开诊所后又退回旧习。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degenerate</td><td>/dɪˈdʒenəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、不及物动词</td><td>衰退的，堕落的；恶化</td><td>degenerate into chaos<br>恶化为混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peaceful protest began to degenerate into shouting and shoving.  和平抗议开始恶化为叫骂和推挤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lapse</td><td>/læps/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>小过失</td><td>a lapse of memory<br>记忆上的小失误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief lapse of memory made him forget his colleague's name entirely.  一时的记忆失误使他完全忘了同事的名字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 recidivate</del></td><td>/rɪˈsɪdəveɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>回到原先的习惯，尤指重新犯罪</td><td>recidivate after release<br>获释后重新犯罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many offenders recidivate within a year of release without proper support.  许多罪犯在缺乏适当支持时会在获释一年内重新犯罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relapse</td><td>/rɪˈlæps/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>重蹈覆辙，再犯</td><td>relapse into old habits<br>重犯旧习</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without support many patients relapse into old habits within months.  缺乏支持的话，许多病人几个月内就重蹈旧习。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retrograde</td><td>/ˈretrəɡreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>倒退的</td><td>a retrograde step<br>一次倒退之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Banning the new textbooks feels like a retrograde step for the whole district.  禁用新教材对整个学区而言像是一次倒退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 retrogress</del></td><td>/ˌrɛtrəˈɡrɛs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>退步，倒退</td><td>retrogress to barbarism<br>倒退回野蛮状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without steady funding the program began to retrogress noticeably.  缺乏稳定资金，这个项目开始明显倒退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 advance</td><td>/ədˈvæns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>前进；促进；预付款</td><td>advance a theory<br>提出一种理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The army advanced slowly through the thick forest at dawn.  黎明时军队缓慢穿过密林前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 progress</td><td>/ˈprɑːˌɡres/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>进步，前进</td><td>make steady progress<br>取得稳步进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students made steady progress over the whole term.  学生们整个学期取得了稳步进展。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>baffle</b></td><td>/ˈbæfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使疑惑</td><td>baffle the experts<br>使专家困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange symptoms continued to baffle the visiting experts.  这些奇怪的症状一直使来访的专家疑惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 befog</del></td><td>/bɪˈfɔːɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使模糊，使困惑</td><td>befog the argument<br>使论证变模糊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Technical jargon befogs the argument instead of making it clearer.  技术术语让论证更模糊，而非更清楚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 befuddle</del></td><td>/bɪˈfʌdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使困惑</td><td>befuddle the audience<br>使听众困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long tables of statistics only befuddle the general audience.  长长的统计表格只会使普通听众困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bewilder</td><td>/bɪˈwɪldər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷惑，使不知所措</td><td>bewilder the newcomers<br>使新来者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The maze of corridors bewildered every visitor on the first day.  迷宫般的走廊让每位来访者第一天都不知所措。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confound</td><td>/kənˈfaʊnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；证明为假，证伪</td><td>confound the critics<br>让批评者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The late victory confounded the critics who had predicted failure.  最后的胜利让预言失败的批评者困惑不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 discombobulate</del></td><td>/ˌdɪskəmˈbɑːbjuleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷惑，使慌乱</td><td>discombobulate the speaker<br>使演讲者慌乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unexpected question completely discombobulated the young candidate.  这个意外的问题让年轻候选人彻底慌了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorient</td><td>/dɪsˈɔːrient/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷失方向，使困惑</td><td>disorient the visitors<br>使来访者迷向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thick fog disoriented the hikers within a few minutes.  浓雾几分钟内就让徒步者失去了方向感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maze</td><td>/ˈmeɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>迷宫；错综复杂</td><td>a maze of corridors<br>迷宫般的走廊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We got lost in a maze of narrow hospital corridors.  我们在迷宫般的狭窄医院走廊里迷了路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mystify</td><td>/ˈmɪstəˌfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑，使迷惑</td><td>mystify the experts<br>使专家们困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden failure of the engine mystified the mechanics.  发动机的突然故障让机械师们十分困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perplex</td><td>/pərˈpleks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑，使费解</td><td>perplex the researchers<br>使研究者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange results perplexed the researchers for several long months.  奇怪的结果让研究者困惑了好几个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 puzzle</td><td>/ˈpʌzəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使困惑；谜题</td><td>puzzle the doctors<br>使医生们困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sudden recovery puzzled the doctors who had treated her.  她的突然康复让治疗过她的医生们感到困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vex</td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 befuddling</td><td>/bɪˈfʌdlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人迷惑的</td><td>befuddling instructions<br>令人迷惑的说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The form came with befuddling instructions in tiny print.  表格附有小字印刷的令人迷惑说明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bewildering</td><td>/bɪˈwɪldərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人困惑的</td><td>a bewildering choice<br>令人眼花的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The menu offered a bewildering choice of desserts.  菜单提供了令人眼花的甜点选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confounding</td><td>/kənˈfaʊndɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人困惑的，混淆的</td><td>a confounding result<br>令人困惑的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trial produced a confounding result that nobody expected.  试验产生了没人预料到的令人困惑结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confusing</td><td>/kənˈfjuːzɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人混乱的，难懂的</td><td>confusing directions<br>令人混乱的指路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His confusing directions sent us the wrong way twice.  他令人混乱的指路让我们走错了两次。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enigmatic</td><td>/ˌenɪɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>an enigmatic smile<br>神秘的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enigmatic answer left the reporters guessing for several days.  她神秘的回答让记者们猜了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 puzzling</td><td>/ˈpʌzəlɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人费解的</td><td>a puzzling silence<br>令人费解的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her puzzling silence worried everyone at the table.  她令人费解的沉默让桌上每个人担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perplexing</td><td>/pərˈpleksɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人困惑的，费解的</td><td>a perplexing problem<br>令人困惑的难题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The engineers faced a perplexing problem with the new engine.  工程师们面对新引擎一个令人困惑的难题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clarify</td><td>/ˈklerɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阐明，澄清</td><td>clarify the rules<br>澄清规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dean clarified the rules after weeks of confused complaints.  在数周混乱的投诉后，院长澄清了规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elucidate</td><td>/iˈluːsɪdeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>阐明</td><td>elucidate the problem<br>阐明问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The footnotes elucidate the problem far better than the chapter itself does.  脚注比正文本身更好地阐明了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apparent</td><td>/əˈperənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的；表面上的</td><td>for no apparent reason<br>没有明显原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It soon became apparent that the machine had never worked.  很快就明显看出这台机器从未运转过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distinct</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰的，明显的</td><td>a distinct impression<br>清晰的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a distinct difference between the two dialects.  这两种方言之间有明显差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 evident</td><td>/ˈevɪdənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显然的</td><td>clearly evident<br>显而易见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her talent for music was evident from a very early age.  她的音乐才华从很小就显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lucid</td><td>/ˈluːsɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达清晰的，易懂的</td><td>a lucid explanation<br>清晰的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor offered a lucid summary of a difficult argument.  教授对一个艰深论证做了清晰的总结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 manifest</td><td>/ˈmænɪfest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>显然的，明显易懂的；显现，显露</td><td>manifest injustice<br>明显的不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His discomfort at the question was manifest to everyone seated around the table.  他对这个问题的不适让围坐桌边的每个人都看得明明白白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 obvious</td><td>/ˈɑːbviəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显而易见的</td><td>an obvious mistake<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The answer seemed obvious once she explained the method.  她解释了方法后，答案似乎显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 patent</td><td>/ˈpeɪtnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>显而易见的，明显的；专利</td><td>a patent lie<br>显而易见的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The excuse was a patent lie and everyone knew it.  这个借口是显而易见的谎言，人人都知道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pellucid</td><td>/pəˈluːsɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰明确的，易懂的</td><td>pellucid prose<br>明白易懂的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pellucid explanation made the theory seem obvious.  他清晰的讲解让这个理论显得浅显易懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 perspicuous</del></td><td>/pərˈspɪkjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表述清晰的，明白易懂的</td><td>a perspicuous style<br>明白易懂的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His writing is perspicuous even when the topic is technical.  即使话题很专业，他的文章也清晰易懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 transparent</td><td>/trænsˈpærənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有歧义的，清晰易懂的</td><td>a transparent explanation<br>清晰易懂的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a transparent explanation that even beginners understood immediately.  他给出清晰易懂的解释，连初学者都立刻明白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bohemian</b></td><td>/boʊˈhiːmiən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>特立独行之人</td><td>a bohemian lifestyle<br>放浪不羁的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young painter led a bohemian lifestyle in a crowded attic studio.  这位年轻画家在拥挤的阁楼画室里过着放浪不羁的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deviant</td><td>/ˈdiːviənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>离常规的人；异常的</td><td>labeled a deviant<br>被视为异类</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Society once treated any deviant thinker as a genuine danger.  社会曾把任何异类思想者视为真正的危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heretic</td><td>/ˈherətɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>异端者，异教徒</td><td>burned as a heretic<br>作为异端被烧死</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In his own time the scientist was treated as a heretic.  在他那个时代，这位科学家被当作异端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 iconoclast</td><td>/aɪˈkɑːnəklæst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>特立独行的人</td><td>a fearless iconoclast<br>无畏的反传统者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a lifelong iconoclast, she questioned every rule the institute cherished.  作为一生特立独行的人，她质疑该研究所珍视的每条规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maverick</td><td>/ˈmævərɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>特立独行之人</td><td>a political maverick<br>政坛的独行者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a political maverick, she voted against her own party on almost every bill.  作为政坛的独行者，她几乎在每项法案上都投票反对本党。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonconformist</td><td>/ˌnɑːnkənˈfɔːrmɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>不墨守成规的人</td><td>a lifelong nonconformist<br>终身的叛逆者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a nonconformist who questioned every rule at school.  他是个不循规蹈矩的人，质疑学校的每条规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 conformer</del></td><td>/kənˈfɔrmər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顺从者，随大流者</td><td>a quiet conformer<br>安静的顺从者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office rewarded the conformer and punished the original thinker.  办公室奖励顺从者，惩罚有独立想法的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conformist</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrmɪst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>墨守成规者，随大流者</td><td>a cautious conformist<br>谨慎的随大流者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In that office every conformist was rewarded and every rebel ignored.  在那个办公室，随大流者受奖，反叛者被无视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bash</b></td><td>/bæʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抨击，严厉批评</td><td>bash the media<br>抨击媒体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Politicians love to bash the press at rallies.  政客们喜欢在集会上抨击新闻界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abuse</td><td>/əˈbjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>辱骂，抨击；不正当或不合理使用</td><td>abuse of power<br>滥用权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was accused of abusing his position for profit.  他被指控滥用职位谋利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assail</td><td>/əˈseɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抨击，严厉批评</td><td>assail a policy<br>抨击某项政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Opponents assailed the plan as reckless and unfair.  反对者抨击该计划轻率且不公。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belabor</td><td>/bɪˈleɪbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>过分强调；痛打</td><td>belabor the point<br>反复纠缠一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is no need to belabor the point any further.  没有必要再对这一点过分强调下去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blast</td><td>/blæst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>爆炸；斥责，抨击</td><td>a bomb blast<br>炸弹爆炸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bomb blast shattered every window on the narrow street.  一次炸弹爆炸震碎了这条窄街上的每扇窗户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 castigate</td><td>/ˈkæstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈（公开）指责</td><td>castigate for negligence<br>因疏忽而受斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge castigated the company for its negligence.  法官严厉指责该公司的疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excoriate</td><td>/ɪkˈskɔːrieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；擦伤皮肤</td><td>excoriate the government<br>严厉批评政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics excoriated the film for its casual cruelty.  批评家严厉批评该片的随意残忍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lambaste</td><td>/læmˈbeɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉斥责，痛骂</td><td>lambaste the government<br>痛斥政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lambasted the film for its shallow, sentimental ending.  批评家严厉斥责这部电影结局浅薄而煽情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savage</td><td>/ˈsævədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>凶残的，野蛮的；猛烈攻击</td><td>a savage beating<br>野蛮的殴打</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics savaged the film for its careless treatment of history.  批评家猛烈批评该片对历史的草率处理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 scathe</del></td><td>/skeɪð/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；伤害</td><td>scathe with bitter sarcasm<br>用讽刺严厉批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers scathed the film for its lazy writing and dull acting.  评论者严厉批评这部电影剧本敷衍、表演乏味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slam</td><td>/ˈslæm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评；猛击</td><td>slam the proposal<br>猛烈批评该提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics slammed the film for its careless plotting.  评论家因情节草率而猛烈批评这部电影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trash</td><td>/ˈtræʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>下层败类；垃圾</td><td>dismiss them as trash<br>视其为下层败类</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her letters show real contempt, calling the newcomers trash.  她的信里满是轻蔑，把新来者称作下层败类。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 vituperate</del></td><td>/vaɪˈtuːpəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谩骂，责骂</td><td>vituperate an opponent<br>谩骂对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist vituperated the mayor in every weekly article.  这位专栏作家在每周的文章里都痛骂市长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acclaim</td><td>/əˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>称赞</td><td>win critical acclaim<br>赢得评论界赞誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won wide critical acclaim across Europe and Asia.  她的第一部小说在欧洲和亚洲赢得了广泛的评论界赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commend</td><td>/kəˈmend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬，表扬</td><td>commend her courage<br>赞扬她的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The principal commended her courage in front of the whole school.  校长在全校面前赞扬了她的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compliment</td><td>/ˈkɑːmplɪmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞；恭维</td><td>pay someone a compliment<br>赞美某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paid her a warm compliment on her new painting.  他对她的新画作说了一句诚挚的赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hail</td><td>/ˈheɪl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冰雹；一阵（如子弹）</td><td>a hail of criticism<br>一阵猛烈的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soldiers advanced through a hail of bullets and smoke.  士兵们在弹雨与烟尘中前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>beholden</b></td><td>/bɪˈhoʊldən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欠他人人情的</td><td>beholden to no one<br>不欠任何人人情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wanted to be beholden to no one for her success.  她希望自己的成功不欠任何人人情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indebted</td><td>/ɪnˈdetɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>感激的；负债的</td><td>deeply indebted to her<br>深深感激她</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I am deeply indebted to her for that timely advice.  我非常感激她那及时的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obligated</td><td>/ˈɑːbləˌɡeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有义务的，不得不的</td><td>feel obligated to help<br>觉得有义务帮忙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt obligated to help her former teacher move house.  她觉得有义务帮旧日老师搬家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obliged</td><td>/əˈblaɪdʒd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>感激的；有义务的</td><td>much obliged to you<br>非常感谢你</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I am much obliged to you for the ride home.  非常感谢你送我回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>behoove</b></td><td>/bɪˈhuːv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>对…有利</td><td>it behooves us to act<br>我们理应行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would behoove the company to answer the letter quickly.  公司尽快回信才对自己有利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 befit</td><td>/bɪˈfɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>适合，与…相称</td><td>befit the occasion<br>与场合相称</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wore a dark suit to befit the solemn occasion.  他穿深色西装以配这个庄重场合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 beseem</del></td><td>/bɪˈsiːm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（旧）适合，相称</td><td>as beseems a scholar<br>一如学者所应有</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such patience beseems a teacher of her long standing.  这样的耐心与她多年的教师身份相称。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fit</td><td>/ˈfɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>相符，适合；合适的</td><td>fit the description<br>符合描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The suspect they arrested did not fit the witness's description.  他们逮捕的嫌疑人并不符合证人的描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 serve</td><td>/ˈsɜːrv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>对…合适，有利于；服务</td><td>serve his purpose<br>合他的用途</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A folding table will serve our purpose well enough.  一张折叠桌就足够合我们的用途。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suite</td><td>/ˈswiːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>套房；一套（家具、组曲）</td><td>a hotel suite<br>酒店套房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delegation stayed in a large suite upstairs.  代表团住在楼上一间大套房里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>beige</b></td><td>/beɪʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏特征的</td><td>a beige personality<br>毫无特色的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report describes him as a beige figure with no strong views.  报告把他描述成一个没有鲜明主张、缺乏特征的人物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 characterless</td><td>/ˈkɛrəktərləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无特色的；缺乏骨气的</td><td>a characterless building<br>毫无特色的建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved into a clean but characterless apartment block.  他们搬进一栋干净却毫无特色的公寓楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faceless</td><td>/ˈfeɪsləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无个性的，无名无姓的</td><td>faceless bureaucrats<br>毫无个性的官僚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Decisions were made by faceless bureaucrats far away.  决定由远处毫无个性的官僚做出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 featureless</td><td>/ˈfiːtʃərləs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏特征的</td><td>a featureless plain<br>毫无特征的平原</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road crossed a flat, featureless plain for eighty miles.  这条路穿过八十英里平坦、毫无特征的平原。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 indistinctive</del></td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋktɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无特色的，难以分辨的</td><td>an indistinctive style<br>毫无特色的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her indistinctive style made the paintings hard to date.  她毫无特色的风格使这些画难以断代。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neutral</td><td>/ˈnuːtrəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>中立的；无刺激性的</td><td>remain neutral in disputes<br>在争端中保持中立</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Switzerland stayed neutral throughout most of the twentieth century.  瑞士在二十世纪大部分时间保持中立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noncommittal</td><td>/ˌnɑːnkəˈmɪtəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不表态的，含糊的</td><td>a noncommittal answer<br>不表态的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister gave a noncommittal answer to every question.  部长对每个问题都给出不表态的回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vanilla</td><td>/vəˈnɪlə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平淡无奇的；香草</td><td>plain vanilla software<br>最普通的软件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chose a plain vanilla version without extra features.  他们选了没有额外功能的最普通版本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diagnostic</td><td>/ˌdaɪəɡˈnɑːstɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特征性的；诊断的</td><td>a diagnostic feature<br>特征性的特点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The narrow leaves are a diagnostic feature of this species.  狭长的叶子是该物种的特征性特点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discriminating</td><td>/dɪsˈkrɪməˌneɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有差别对待的；有鉴别力的</td><td>policies discriminating against women<br>歧视女性的政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court struck down policies discriminating against women in hiring.  法院废除了在招聘中歧视女性的政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distinct</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰的，明显的</td><td>a distinct impression<br>清晰的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a distinct difference between the two dialects.  这两种方言之间有明显差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distinctive</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋktɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>特征性的，典型的</td><td>a distinctive style<br>独特的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her distinctive style makes each painting recognizable from across the gallery.  她独特的风格使每幅画在展厅另一端就能被认出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distinguishing</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有区别性的，特征性的</td><td>a distinguishing mark<br>识别标记</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bird's red crest is its main distinguishing mark.  这种鸟的红冠是它主要的识别标记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 identifying</td><td>/aɪˈdentəˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>用于辨认的，标识性的</td><td>identifying details<br>身份识别信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report removed all identifying details of the patients.  报告删去了病人所有身份识别信息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peculiar</td><td>/pəˈkjuːliər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；特有的</td><td>a peculiar smell<br>一股怪味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a peculiar smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股奇怪的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 typical</td><td>/ˈtɪpəkəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>典型的，一贯的</td><td>a typical example<br>典型的例子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This building is a typical example of the local style.  这栋建筑是当地风格的典型代表。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bureaucratic</b></td><td>/ˌbjʊrəˈkrætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>官僚主义的</td><td>bureaucratic red tape<br>官僚的繁琐手续</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Endless bureaucratic delays kept the clinic from opening on time.  没完没了的官僚拖延使诊所无法按时开业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 governmental</td><td>/ˌɡʌvərnˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>政府的，官方的</td><td>governmental agencies<br>政府机构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several governmental agencies joined the flood investigation.  几个政府机构参与了洪灾调查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complicated</td><td>/ˈkɑːmplɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂的，难懂的</td><td>a complicated process<br>复杂的流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax rules are so complicated that most people hire help.  税务规定如此复杂，多数人都请人帮忙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>belligerent</b></td><td>/bəˈlɪdʒərənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，好战的</td><td>a belligerent tone<br>好斗的口气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the question in a belligerent tone that shocked us.  他用一种令我们震惊的好斗口气回答了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggressive</td><td>/əˈɡresɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的；积极进取的，强有力的</td><td>an aggressive marketing campaign<br>强势的营销活动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog became aggressive whenever strangers approached the gate.  只要有陌生人靠近大门，这只狗就变得有攻击性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agonistic</td><td>/ˌæɡəˈnɪstɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好争斗的，对抗性的</td><td>agonistic behavior<br>对抗性行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The study measured agonistic behavior among captive wolves.  该研究测量了圈养狼之间的对抗性行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assaultive</td><td>/əˈsɔːltɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好攻击的，暴力的</td><td>assaultive behavior<br>暴力攻击行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient's assaultive behavior required constant supervision by trained staff.  这名病人的攻击性行为需要受过训练的人员持续看护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bellicose</td><td>/ˈbeləˌkoʊs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，挑起争斗的</td><td>bellicose rhetoric<br>好战的言辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general's bellicose rhetoric alarmed the neighboring countries.  这位将军的好战言辞令邻国警觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 combative</td><td>/kəmˈbætɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，好争辩的</td><td>a combative tone<br>好斗的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered every question in a combative tone.  他用好斗的语气回答每一个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contentious</td><td>/kənˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引起争论的；好争论的，好战的</td><td>a contentious issue<br>有争议的议题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Land ownership remains the most contentious issue in the peace negotiations.  土地所有权仍是和谈中最有争议的议题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disputatious</del></td><td>/ˌdɪspjuˈteɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好争辩的，爱吵架的</td><td>a disputatious committee<br>好争辩的委员会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The disputatious committee argued for hours without reaching any decision.  这个好争辩的委员会争了几小时也没有决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feisty</td><td>/ˈfaɪsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，好争论的</td><td>a feisty debater<br>好争辩的辩手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The feisty debater challenged every point her opponent made.  这位好斗的辩手对对手的每个论点都提出挑战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gladiatorial</td><td>/ˌɡlædiəˈtɔːriəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>角斗士的；激烈对抗的</td><td>a gladiatorial contest<br>角斗式的较量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate turned into a gladiatorial contest of egos.  辩论变成了一场自负者之间的角斗式较量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 militant</td><td>/ˈmɪlətənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>激进好战的；激进分子</td><td>a militant activist<br>激进的活动人士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union took a militant stance in the pay talks.  工会在薪资谈判中采取了强硬立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pugnacious</td><td>/pʌɡˈneɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好斗的，爱争吵的</td><td>a pugnacious debater<br>好争辩的辩手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pugnacious little dog barked at every passing car.  这只好斗的小狗对每辆过路的车都叫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quarrelsome</td><td>/ˈkwɔːrəlsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好争吵的</td><td>a quarrelsome neighbor<br>爱争吵的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their quarrelsome neighbor complained about absolutely everything.  他们爱争吵的邻居对一切都抱怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 truculent</td><td>/ˈtrʌkjələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，好斗的；粗野的</td><td>a truculent response<br>咄咄逼人的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truculent teenager argued with every teacher who tried to correct him.  这个好斗的少年跟每位试图纠正他的老师都吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 warlike</td><td>/ˈwɔːrˌlaɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，尚武的</td><td>a warlike nation<br>好战的国家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ancient writers portrayed the tribe as fiercely warlike.  古代作家把这个部族描写得极其好战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggressiveness</td><td>/əˈɡresɪvnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好斗，侵略性</td><td>unusual aggressiveness<br>异常的好斗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog's aggressiveness alarmed everyone in the park.  那条狗的好斗让公园里所有人警觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 assaultiveness</del></td><td>/əˈsɔːltɪvnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>攻击性，好动武</td><td>growing assaultiveness<br>日益增长的攻击性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nurses noted the patient's growing assaultiveness at night.  护士注意到病人夜间攻击性日增。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bellicosity</del></td><td>/ˌbelɪˈkɑːsɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好战，好斗</td><td>open bellicosity<br>公开的好战</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general's bellicosity alarmed even his own allies.  将军的好战甚至使他自己的盟友不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 combativeness</td><td>/kəmˈbætɪvnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好斗，斗志强</td><td>fierce combativeness<br>强烈的斗志</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her combativeness in court intimidated younger lawyers.  她在法庭上的斗志让年轻律师畏惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 contentiousness</del></td><td>/kənˈtenʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好争论，爱争执</td><td>needless contentiousness<br>不必要的争执</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His contentiousness made every committee meeting run long.  他的好争论让每次委员会会议都超时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disputatiousness</del></td><td>/dɪˌspjuːˈteɪʃəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好争辩</td><td>academic disputatiousness<br>学术上的好争辩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The seminar was famous for its cheerful disputatiousness.  这门讨论课以愉快的好争辩闻名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 feistiness</del></td><td>/ˈfaɪstinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好斗，倔强有劲</td><td>surprising feistiness<br>出人意料的倔劲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small terrier's feistiness surprised the larger dogs.  小猎狐犬的倔劲让大狗吃惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 militance</del></td><td>/ˈmɪlɪtəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>战斗性，好战</td><td>rising militance<br>日益高涨的战斗性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The union's militance grew sharply after the wage cuts.  减薪后工会的战斗性急剧增强。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pugnacity</del></td><td>/pʌɡˈnæsɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好斗，喜欢争斗</td><td>natural pugnacity<br>天生的好斗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His natural pugnacity served him well as a boxer.  他天生的好斗使他成为出色的拳手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 quarrelsomeness</del></td><td>/ˈkwɔːrəlsəmnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好争吵</td><td>constant quarrelsomeness<br>不断的争吵习性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their quarrelsomeness ruined nearly every family dinner.  他们的好争吵几乎破坏了每次家庭晚餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 scrappiness</del></td><td>/ˈskræpinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好斗，拼劲</td><td>youthful scrappiness<br>年轻人的拼劲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team's scrappiness made up for its lack of size.  这支球队的拼劲弥补了身材上的不足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 truculence</del></td><td>/ˈtrʌkjələns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好斗，凶蛮</td><td>open truculence<br>公开的凶蛮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the reporters with unexpected truculence.  他以出人意料的凶蛮回应记者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dove</td><td>/ˈdʌv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>主和派，鸽派；鸽子</td><td>hawks and doves<br>鹰派与鸽派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabinet split between hawks and doves over the treaty.  内阁就该条约分裂为鹰派与鸽派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacific</td><td>/pəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平和的，爱好和平的</td><td>a pacific temperament<br>平和的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He settled the quarrel with a few pacific words.  他用几句平和的话平息了争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peaceful</td><td>/ˈpiːsfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和平的，平静的</td><td>a peaceful settlement<br>和平的解决方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides finally chose a peaceful path out of conflict.  双方最终选择了走出冲突的和平道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacifism</td><td>/ˈpæsɪˌfɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>和平主义，反战主义</td><td>lifelong pacifism<br>终生的和平主义</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her pacifism kept her out of the army entirely.  她的和平主义使她完全不参军。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>beset</b></td><td>/bɪˈset/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使苦恼，骚扰；攻击，袭击</td><td>beset by doubts<br>为疑虑所困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young author was beset by doubts about his second novel.  这位年轻作者被对第二部小说的疑虑所困扰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agonize</td><td>/ˈæɡənaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）非常痛苦，苦苦思索</td><td>agonize over a decision<br>为决定苦苦纠结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She agonized for weeks over whether to accept the job.  她为是否接受这份工作苦恼了好几周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 anguish</td><td>/ˈæŋɡwɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>极度痛苦，苦恼</td><td>in great anguish<br>极度痛苦之中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His face showed the anguish of a sleepless night.  他的脸上写着一夜无眠的痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bedevil</td><td>/bɪˈdevəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使苦恼，困扰</td><td>bedevil the project<br>困扰这个项目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Delays have bedeviled the project from the very beginning.  延误从一开始就困扰着这个项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 besiege</td><td>/bɪˈsiːdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>围攻；不断烦扰</td><td>besiege the city<br>围攻城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters besieged the minister with questions about the scandal.  记者们就丑闻不断向部长发问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curse</td><td>/ˈkɜːrs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>诅咒，咒骂</td><td>curse one's enemies<br>诅咒自己的敌人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old woman cursed her enemies in a trembling voice.  老妇人用颤抖的声音诅咒她的敌人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 excruciate</del></td><td>/ɪkˈskruːʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>折磨，使痛苦</td><td>excruciate the prisoners<br>折磨囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The interrogators excruciated the prisoners for information they never had.  审讯者折磨囚犯，逼问他们从未掌握的情报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harrow</td><td>/ˈhæroʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>折磨，使苦恼</td><td>harrow his conscience<br>折磨他的良心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The memory of that night harrowed him for the rest of his life.  那一夜的记忆折磨了他的余生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 persecute</td><td>/ˈpɜːrsɪkjuːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>迫害，折磨</td><td>persecute religious minorities<br>迫害宗教少数群体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime persecuted religious minorities for more than a decade.  该政权迫害宗教少数群体十多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plague</td><td>/pleɪɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>瘟疫；折磨，使…痛苦</td><td>plagued by doubts<br>被疑虑折磨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doubts about the decision plagued him for the rest of the year.  对那个决定的疑虑折磨了他余下的一整年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rack</td><td>/ˈræk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>折磨，使痛苦；架子</td><td>racked with pain<br>受痛苦折磨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was racked by guilt long after the incident was forgotten.  事件被淡忘后他仍长久受负罪感折磨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torment</td><td>/tɔːrˈment/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>折磨，使痛苦；痛苦</td><td>torment the prisoners<br>折磨囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guilt tormented him for years after the accident.  事故之后，负罪感折磨了他多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torture</td><td>/ˈtɔːrtʃər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>折磨，拷打；酷刑</td><td>under torture<br>在酷刑之下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long wait for results was pure torture for her.  漫长等待结果对她来说简直是折磨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assail</td><td>/əˈseɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抨击，严厉批评</td><td>assail a policy<br>抨击某项政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Opponents assailed the plan as reckless and unfair.  反对者抨击该计划轻率且不公。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assault</td><td>/əˈsɔːlt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>攻击，袭击</td><td>launch an assault<br>发动攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Troops launched a night assault on the fortress.  部队对要塞发动了夜间进攻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attack</td><td>/əˈtæk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>攻击，抨击</td><td>attack his motives<br>攻击他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rival scholars attacked the credibility of her new evidence.  对立的学者抨击她新证据的可信度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charge</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːrdʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>指控；费用；充电</td><td>face criminal charges<br>面临刑事指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prosecutor charged him with fraud after months of investigation.  经过数月调查，检察官指控他欺诈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 raid</td><td>/ˈreɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>突袭，袭击</td><td>an air raid<br>一次空袭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soldiers raided the camp shortly after midnight.  士兵在午夜过后不久突袭了营地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rush</td><td>/rʌʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>匆忙，急速行动；催促</td><td>rush to the airport<br>赶往机场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They rushed to the airport and barely caught the flight.  他们赶往机场，勉强赶上了航班。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 storm</td><td>/ˈstɔːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暴风雨；一阵猛烈（如抗议）</td><td>a storm of protest<br>一阵抗议风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision caused a storm of protest on social media.  这一决定在社交媒体上引发抗议风暴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strike</td><td>/straɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>攻击；撞击</td><td>strike a heavy blow<br>猛击一下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boxer tried to strike a heavy blow before the bell rang.  拳手想在铃响前猛击一拳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 set on</td><td>/set ˈɑːn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>攻击，袭击</td><td>set on a stranger<br>袭击陌生人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard dogs were set on the intruder the moment he climbed in.  他一翻进来，看守犬就被放出去攻击他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comfort</td><td>/ˈkʌmfərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>舒适；安慰</td><td>live in comfort<br>过舒适的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old chair gave him a great deal of comfort.  那把旧椅子给他带来极大的舒适。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 console</td><td>/kənˈsoʊl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，慰藉</td><td>console a grieving friend<br>安慰悲伤的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing anyone said could console her that evening.  那天晚上无论谁说什么都无法安慰她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 solace</td><td>/ˈsɑːləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>安慰</td><td>find solace in music<br>在音乐中找到安慰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found solace in music during the long months of her illness.  在漫长的病中，她在音乐里找到安慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 defend</td><td>/dɪˈfend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>保卫，辩护</td><td>defend the border<br>保卫边境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soldiers defended the bridge for three days.  士兵们守卫这座桥三天之久。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 guard</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>保护；警卫</td><td>guard against loss<br>防范损失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two dogs guard the warehouse every night.  两条狗每晚守卫这座仓库。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 protect</td><td>/prəˈtekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>保护，防护</td><td>protect from harm<br>保护免受伤害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick gloves protect your hands from the cold.  厚手套保护你的双手免受寒冷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shield</td><td>/ˈʃiːld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>保护，遮挡；盾</td><td>shield one's eyes<br>遮住眼睛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised a hand to shield her eyes from the glare.  她举手遮挡刺眼的强光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bewitching</b></td><td>/bɪˈwɪtʃɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的；令人着迷的</td><td>a bewitching smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the photographer a bewitching smile over her shoulder.  她回头对摄影师露出迷人的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alluring</td><td>/əˈlʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诱人的，有魅力的</td><td>an alluring smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The alluring aroma of fresh bread drew us into the bakery.  新鲜面包诱人的香气把我们引进了面包店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appealing</td><td>/əˈpiːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的</td><td>an appealing offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made an appealing offer that she could hardly refuse.  公司提出了一个她几乎无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attractive</td><td>/əˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，迷人的</td><td>an attractive offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made him an attractive offer he could not refuse.  公司给了他一个无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 captivating</td><td>/ˈkæptɪˌveɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，令人着迷的</td><td>a captivating performance<br>令人着迷的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her captivating voice held the entire audience in complete silence.  她迷人的歌声让全场观众鸦雀无声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charismatic</td><td>/ˌkærɪzˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有魅力的，有感召力的</td><td>a charismatic leader<br>有感召力的领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charismatic speaker convinced thousands to join his new movement.  这位有魅力的演讲者说服数千人加入他的新运动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchanting</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃæntɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，醉人的</td><td>an enchanting melody<br>醉人的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden looked enchanting under the soft light of lanterns.  花园在灯笼柔和的光线下显得十分迷人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entrancing</td><td>/ɪnˈtrænsɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人入迷的，使人陶醉的</td><td>an entrancing dance<br>令人入迷的舞蹈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer's entrancing movements kept every eye fixed on the stage.  舞者令人入迷的动作让所有目光都盯着舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glamorous</td><td>/ˈɡlæmərəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的</td><td>a glamorous lifestyle<br>迷人的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left her quiet hometown for a glamorous life in Paris.  她离开宁静的家乡，去巴黎过迷人的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luring</td><td>/ˈlʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引诱的，诱惑的</td><td>the luring promise of wealth<br>财富的诱人承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Advertisers use luring images to make ordinary products seem exciting.  广告商用诱人的图片让普通产品显得令人兴奋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnetic</td><td>/mæɡˈnetɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有魅力的；有磁性的</td><td>a magnetic personality<br>有魅力的个性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His magnetic personality filled the small room instantly.  他有魅力的个性瞬间充满了那个小房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seductive</td><td>/sɪˈdʌktɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诱人的，有诱惑力的</td><td>a seductive argument<br>诱人的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The idea of easy money is seductive but usually dangerous.  轻松赚钱的想法很诱人，但通常很危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repellent</td><td>/rɪˈpelənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>令人厌恶的；驱虫剂</td><td>a repellent odor<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found his arrogant remarks utterly repellent and left early.  我觉得他傲慢的言论令人极其厌恶，于是提前离开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repelling</td><td>/rəˈpelɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人反感的，令人厌恶的</td><td>a repelling appearance<br>令人反感的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swamp gave off a repelling smell of rotting plants.  沼泽散发出腐烂植物令人厌恶的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repugnant</td><td>/rɪˈpʌɡnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>morally repugnant behavior<br>道德上可憎的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most readers found the cruelty of that character morally repugnant and unforgivable.  多数读者觉得那个角色的残忍在道德上令人厌恶，不可宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repulsive</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a repulsive sight<br>令人恶心的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rotting fish on the beach was absolutely repulsive.  沙滩上腐烂的鱼令人极其恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revolting</td><td>/rɪˈvoʊltɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a revolting smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A revolting smell came from the abandoned refrigerator in the yard.  院里那台废弃冰箱散出一股恶心的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>blackmail</b></td><td>/ˈblækmeɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>敲诈，勒索</td><td>blackmail a witness<br>敲诈证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tried to blackmail the witness into staying silent.  他们试图敲诈证人让其保持沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extortion</td><td>/ekˈstɔːrʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>敲诈，勒索</td><td>charged with extortion<br>被控敲诈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two city officials were charged with extortion last week.  两名市政官员上周被控敲诈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bluff</b></td><td>/blʌf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>虚张声势地骗人，唬人</td><td>bluff your way through<br>靠虚张声势混过去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to bluff his way through the whole interview.  他试图靠虚张声势混过整场面试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abrupt</td><td>/əˈbrʌpt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>突然的；言语生硬的</td><td>an abrupt reply<br>生硬的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His abrupt answer ended the conversation before it really began.  他生硬的回答让谈话还没开始就结束了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brusque</td><td>/brʌsk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>唐突无礼的，简慢的</td><td>a brusque manner<br>唐突的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clerk's brusque manner offended nearly every waiting customer.  那名职员唐突的态度几乎冒犯了所有等候的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crusty</td><td>/ˈkrʌsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>脾气坏的，粗率的；有硬皮的</td><td>a crusty old sailor<br>脾气粗率的老水手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crusty old sailor barked orders at everyone on deck.  那个脾气粗率的老水手对甲板上所有人吼命令。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curt</td><td>/kɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>言词简略的，直接（以至显得粗鲁）的</td><td>a curt answer<br>简慢的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her curt reply made it clear that the discussion was over.  她简慢的回答清楚表明讨论已经结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 downright</td><td>/ˈdaʊnˌraɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，彻底的</td><td>downright dangerous<br>十足危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That suggestion is downright dangerous and should be dropped.  那个建议完全是危险的，应该放弃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forthright</td><td>/ˈfɔːrθraɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>直率的，说话率真的；直接的，不绕弯子的</td><td>a forthright answer<br>直率的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His forthright answer surprised the reporters who expected evasion.  他直率的回答让原本预期他闪避的记者很意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snippy</td><td>/ˈsnɪpi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>说话尖刻的，唐突无礼的</td><td>a snippy reply<br>尖刻的回复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sent a snippy reply and regretted it an hour later.  她发了一条尖刻的回复，一小时后就后悔了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 straightforward</td><td>/ˌstreɪtˈfɔːrwərd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单明了的；坦率的</td><td>a straightforward procedure<br>简单直接的流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The instructions are straightforward enough for a child to follow.  说明简单明了，连孩子都能照着做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unceremonious</td><td>/ˌʌnserəˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>唐突无礼的，草率的</td><td>an unceremonious dismissal<br>唐突的解职</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unceremonious exit surprised the guests still waiting for dessert.  他唐突离场，让还等着甜点的客人吃了一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bamboozle</td><td>/bæmˈbuzəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，蒙骗</td><td>bamboozle the customers<br>欺骗顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman bamboozled them into buying a worthless extended warranty.  推销员骗他们买了毫无价值的延保。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beguile</td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；使着迷</td><td>beguile the audience<br>令观众着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician could beguile an audience for a full hour.  这位魔术师能让观众着迷整整一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cozen</td><td>/ˈkʌzn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>cozen him into agreeing<br>诱骗他同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler cozened elderly widows out of their modest life savings.  那个骗子诱骗年老的寡妇们交出微薄的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
