<tr><td>↳近 deceive</td><td>/dɪˈsiːv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，误导</td><td>deceive the public<br>欺骗公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He deceived his investors with promises of quick and easy profits.  他用轻松快速获利的承诺欺骗了投资者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delude</td><td>/dɪˈluːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，使误信</td><td>delude oneself<br>自欺欺人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not delude yourself into thinking the danger has passed.  别欺骗自己，以为危险已经过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dupe</td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fool</td><td>/ˈfuːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>傻瓜，蠢人；愚弄</td><td>make a fool of oneself<br>让自己出丑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt like a fool after believing the obvious lie.  信了那个明显的谎言后，他觉得自己像个傻瓜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoax</td><td>/hoʊks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>an elaborate hoax<br>精心设计的骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph turned out to be an elaborate hoax created by students.  那张照片原来是学生们精心设计的骗局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misguide</td><td>/mɪsˈɡaɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误导，使误入歧途</td><td>misguide the public<br>误导公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The advertisement was designed to misguide careless buyers.  这则广告旨在误导粗心的买家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misinform</td><td>/ˌmɪsɪnˈfɔːrm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误告，提供错误信息</td><td>misinform the public<br>误导公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone misinformed us about the museum's closing time last Saturday.  上周六有人把博物馆的关门时间告错了我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mislead</td><td>/mɪsˈliːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误导，使误信</td><td>mislead investors<br>误导投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chart was designed to mislead careless readers.  这张图表意在误导不细心的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trick</td><td>/ˈtrɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；诡计</td><td>trick someone into paying<br>诱骗某人付钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She tricked him into revealing the password to the account.  她骗他说出了账户密码。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circuitous</td><td>/sərˈkjuːɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回的，不直接的</td><td>a circuitous route<br>迂回的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a circuitous route to avoid the flooded highway.  我们绕了一条迂回的路以避开被淹的公路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mealy-mouthed</td><td>/ˈmiːli ˈmaʊðd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>说话拐弯抹角的</td><td>a mealy-mouthed apology<br>含糊其词的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters distrust mealy-mouthed politicians who never give a straight answer.  选民不信任那些从不直接回答、说话含糊的政客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disabuse</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈbjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打消错误念头，纠正</td><td>disabuse him of the notion<br>打消他的错误念头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took hours to disabuse him of the notion that the test was simple.  花了好几个小时才打消他认为考试很简单的错误念头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 disenchant</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsɪnˈtʃænt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使清醒</td><td>disenchant the young voters<br>使年轻选民清醒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years in politics disenchanted her with grand promises.  多年从政使她对宏大承诺不再抱幻想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disillusion</td><td>/ˌdɪsɪˈluːʒən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使醒悟，使幻想破灭</td><td>disillusion his followers<br>让追随者幻想破灭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The leaked memo disillusioned even loyal employees.  泄露的备忘录让连忠诚的员工都幻想破灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bogus</b></td><td>/ˈboʊɡəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>伪造的，假冒的</td><td>a bogus claim<br>虚假的索赔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police discovered a stack of bogus passports in the van.  警方在货车里发现一叠伪造护照。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 counterfeit</td><td>/ˈkaʊntərfɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>仿制的，假冒的；仿制</td><td>counterfeit banknotes<br>伪造的钞票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police seized thousands of counterfeit banknotes hidden in the delivery van.  警方缴获了藏在送货车里的数千张假钞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake</td><td>/ˈfeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>假的，伪造的；伪造品</td><td>a fake diploma<br>假文凭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery unknowingly displayed a fake for almost ten years.  这家画廊不知情地展出了一件赝品近十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 false</td><td>/ˈfɔːls/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚假的，不真诚的</td><td>a false smile<br>虚假的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His false praise fooled nobody at the small dinner table.  他虚假的赞美在那张小餐桌上骗不了任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forged</td><td>/ˈfɔːrdʒd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>伪造的，假冒的</td><td>a forged signature<br>伪造的签名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bank noticed a forged signature on the withdrawal slip.  银行发现取款单上有伪造的签名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inauthentic</td><td>/ˌɪnɔːˈθentɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不真实的，伪造的</td><td>an inauthentic signature<br>伪造的签名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts declared the painting inauthentic after careful tests.  专家经仔细检测宣布这幅画并非真品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phony</td><td>/ˈfoʊni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>假的，冒充的；骗子</td><td>a phony accent<br>假装的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used a phony name when renting the small apartment.  他租那间小公寓时用了假名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 queer</td><td>/ˈkwɪr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>古怪的，奇特的</td><td>a queer feeling<br>奇怪的感觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A queer feeling told her the house was empty.  一种奇怪的感觉告诉她房子是空的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sham</td><td>/ʃæm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>掩饰，假装；虚假的</td><td>a sham marriage<br>假婚姻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple admitted their marriage was a sham arranged for the visa.  这对夫妻承认他们的婚姻是为签证安排的假婚姻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snide</td><td>/ˈsnaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假冒的；含讥讽的</td><td>snide remarks<br>讥讽的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made a snide comment about her cheap watch.  他对她廉价的手表说了句讥讽的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spurious</td><td>/ˈspjʊriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假的，伪造的</td><td>spurious claims<br>虚假的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The website spread spurious claims about the new vaccine.  该网站传播关于新疫苗的虚假说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unauthentic</td><td>/ˌʌnɔˈθentɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不真实的，非原作的</td><td>an unauthentic signature<br>一个不真实的签名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts declared the painting unauthentic after careful chemical testing.  专家经仔细化学检测后宣布这幅画并非真品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 authentic</td><td>/ɔːˈθentɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，非仿造的</td><td>authentic Italian cuisine<br>正宗的意大利菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum confirmed the letter was an authentic original.  博物馆确认这封信是真实的原件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genuine</td><td>/ˈdʒenjəwən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真正的，真实的；真诚的</td><td>genuine leather<br>真皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts confirmed that the signature on the letter was genuine.  专家确认信上的签名是真的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 real</td><td>/ˈriːl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，真正的</td><td>a real event<br>真实事件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is based on a real event from 1943.  这部电影基于1943年的一件真实事件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unfaked</del></td><td>/ʌnˈfeɪkt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，未伪造的</td><td>unfaked emotion<br>真实未做作的情感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her surprise was clearly unfaked when the door opened.  门打开时她的惊讶显然不是假装的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bona fide</td><td>/ˌboʊnə ˈfaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，真实的，善意的</td><td>a bona fide offer<br>真诚的报价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only bona fide students may borrow books from this library.  只有真正的在校学生才能从本馆借书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bore</b></td><td>/bɔːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>令人厌烦的人或事物</td><td>an unbearable old bore<br>让人受不了的老唠叨鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My uncle is such a bore that guests leave early.  我叔叔特别无趣，客人们都早早离席。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drip</td><td>/ˈdrɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>令人厌烦的乏味之人；滴落</td><td>a boring old drip<br>讨厌的乏味之人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They called him a drip because he never joined in.  他们叫他闷蛋，因为他从不参与。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 droner</del></td><td>/ˈdroʊnər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>说话单调乏味的人</td><td>a monotonous droner<br>单调乏味的讲话者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecturer was a droner who slowly emptied the hall.  那位讲师说话单调，把礼堂讲空了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snoozer</td><td>/ˈsnuːzər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>令人昏睡的人或事</td><td>a real snoozer<br>十足的催眠货</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The second half of the film was a real snoozer.  这部电影的后半段十足催眠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 yawner</del></td><td>/ˈjɔːnər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>令人厌倦乏味的事物</td><td>a complete yawner<br>完全乏味的东西</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate turned into a complete yawner by midnight.  到午夜辩论变得完全乏味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jade</td><td>/ˈdʒeɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使厌倦，使疲惫；玉</td><td>jaded by routine<br>被日常琐事弄厌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of endless touring had jaded the young singer.  多年不断的巡演使这位年轻歌手厌倦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pall</td><td>/pɔːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>令人厌倦，失去吸引力</td><td>begin to pall<br>开始令人生厌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novelty of city life began to pall after his first lonely winter.  在孤独的第一个冬天过后，城市生活的新鲜感开始让他生厌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tire</td><td>/ˈtaɪər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使疲劳，感到疲倦</td><td>tire easily<br>容易疲倦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long meetings tire her far more than a hard morning run.  长会比清晨的剧烈跑步更让她疲倦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weary</td><td>/ˈwɪri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>令人厌倦的；疲倦的</td><td>a weary routine<br>令人厌倦的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The weary meetings dragged on with no decision in sight.  这些令人厌倦的会议一拖再拖，看不到任何决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absorb</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使全神贯注；吸收</td><td>be absorbed in the book<br>沉浸在书里</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puzzle absorbed him completely for the whole afternoon.  这道谜题让他整个下午都全神贯注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engage</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>雇用，聘请；从事，参与</td><td>engage a lawyer<br>聘请一位律师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They engaged a local guide to lead them through the forest.  他们聘请了一位当地导游带他们穿过森林。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engross</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使全神贯注</td><td>engross the young reader<br>使小读者全神贯注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The adventure novel engrossed the young reader for an entire rainy weekend.  这本冒险小说让小读者整个雨天的周末都全神贯注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enthrall</td><td>/ɪnˈθrɔːl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（像用魔咒般）吸引</td><td>enthrall the entire audience<br>吸引住全场观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician enthralled the entire audience with a single deck of cards.  魔术师仅用一副牌就吸引住了全场观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fascinate</td><td>/ˈfæsɪneɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈吸引，使入迷</td><td>fascinate young readers<br>使小读者着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stories about strange deep-sea creatures fascinate young readers all over the world.  关于奇异深海生物的故事让全世界的小读者着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grip</td><td>/ˈɡrɪp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吸引住；紧握</td><td>grip the reader's attention<br>抓住读者注意力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film gripped us from the very first scene.  这部电影从第一个镜头就抓住了我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interest</td><td>/ˈɪntrəst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>兴趣，关注；利息</td><td>show interest in music<br>对音乐表现出兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She lost interest in the project after only a month.  仅一个月后她就对这个项目失去了兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intrigue</td><td>/ˈɪntriːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>阴谋；激起…的兴趣</td><td>political intrigue<br>政治阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Court records reveal decades of political intrigue behind the sudden fall of the minister.  法庭记录揭示了这位大臣突然倒台背后数十年的政治阴谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brandish</b></td><td>/ˈbrændɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（带有威胁性地）挥舞</td><td>brandish a sword<br>挥舞长剑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The angry pirate began to brandish a sword above his head.  愤怒的海盗开始在头顶挥舞长剑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shake</td><td>/ˈʃeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>挥动；摇动</td><td>shake a fist<br>挥动拳头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He shook his fist angrily at the departing taxi.  他愤怒地朝驶离的出租车挥拳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swing</td><td>/ˈswɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>转向，摆动；秋千</td><td>swing sharply to the left<br>猛地转向左侧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bus swung left to avoid the fallen branch.  公交车向左急转以避开落下的树枝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wave</td><td>/ˈweɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>挥手示意；波浪</td><td>wave goodbye<br>挥手告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He waved to the driver to stop at the corner.  他向司机挥手示意在拐角停下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 drop</td><td>/ˈdrɑːp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>下降，减少；落下</td><td>drop sharply<br>急剧下降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales dropped by half after the price increase.  涨价后销量下降了一半。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>brim</b></td><td>/brɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>边缘，口沿；注满，充满</td><td>brim with confidence<br>充满自信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her eyes brimmed with tears as she read the old letter.  她读那封旧信时，眼里满是泪水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 borderline</td><td>/ˈbɔːrdərlaɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>边界，界线；界线上的</td><td>on the borderline<br>处在界线上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village sits right on the borderline between two provinces.  这个村子正好位于两省的边界上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bound</td><td>/baʊnd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>界限；决心的，一定会的</td><td>within the bounds of law<br>在法律范围内</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her curiosity knew no bounds when she entered the old library.  她走进那座旧图书馆时，好奇心毫无边界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boundary</td><td>/ˈbaʊndəri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>边界，界限</td><td>the boundary between fields<br>田地之间的边界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A low stone wall marks the boundary between the farms.  一道矮石墙标出两个农场的边界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 circumference</td><td>/sərˈkʌmfərəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>圆周，周长</td><td>the circumference of the circle<br>圆的周长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Measure the circumference of the trunk at chest height.  在胸高处测量树干的周长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confines</td><td>/ˈkɑːnˌfaɪnz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>范围，界限</td><td>the confines of the campus<br>校园的范围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students must stay within the confines of the campus.  学生必须待在校园范围内。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 edge</td><td>/ˈedʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>边缘，边沿</td><td>the edge of the cliff<br>悬崖边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stood at the edge of the cliff and looked down.  她站在悬崖边缘往下看。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frame</td><td>/ˈfreɪm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>制定，构想；框架</td><td>frame a new policy<br>制定新政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee framed a new policy on remote work overnight.  委员会一夜之间制定了新的远程工作政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fringe</td><td>/frɪndʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>边缘</td><td>on the fringe of society<br>处于社会边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel describes families living on the fringe of society.  这部小说描写生活在社会边缘的家庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 margin</td><td>/ˈmɑːrdʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>边缘；页边空白；差额</td><td>the margin of the lake<br>湖的边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tall reeds grow along the margin of the lake.  高高的芦苇沿着湖边生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perimeter</td><td>/pəˈrɪmɪtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>边界，界限</td><td>guard the perimeter<br>守卫外围边界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soldiers guarded the perimeter of the camp throughout the night.  士兵们整夜守卫着营地的周边界线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 periphery</td><td>/pəˈrɪfəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>外围，边缘</td><td>the periphery of the city<br>城市的外围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New factories sprang up on the periphery of the city.  新工厂在城市外围拔地而起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skirt</td><td>/skɜːrt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>绕行，避开</td><td>skirt the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister tried to skirt the issue with a long, vague answer.  部长试图用冗长含糊的回答回避这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 verge</td><td>/ˈvɜːrdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>边缘，边界</td><td>on the verge of collapse<br>在崩溃的边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company was on the verge of collapse last winter.  去年冬天这家公司濒临倒闭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bristle</td><td>/ˈbrɪsl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>愤然发怒，恼怒</td><td>bristle at the suggestion<br>对该提议大为不满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bristled at the suggestion that his research was careless.  有人说他的研究草率，他顿时怒不可遏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bulge</td><td>/bʌldʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>凸起</td><td>a bulge in his pocket<br>他口袋里的鼓包</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A strange bulge in his jacket pocket caught the officer's attention.  他夹克口袋里奇怪的鼓包引起了警官的注意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burst</td><td>/ˈbɜːrst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>爆裂；突发，爆发</td><td>burst into flames<br>突然燃起大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old pipe burst and flooded the basement within minutes.  旧管子爆裂，几分钟就淹了地下室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bustle</td><td>/ˈbʌsl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>忙乱，喧闹；（快速地）行走，奔忙</td><td>the bustle of the street<br>街上的喧闹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I love the bustle of the market on Saturday mornings.  我喜欢周六早晨市场上的喧闹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 buzz</td><td>/ˈbʌz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嗡嗡叫；嘈杂声</td><td>buzz around the flowers<br>在花丛间嗡嗡飞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bees buzzed lazily above the clover.  蜜蜂在三叶草上懒懒地嗡嗡飞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crawl</td><td>/ˈkrɔːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢行进；爬行</td><td>crawl along the highway<br>在公路上缓慢行进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crawled along the flooded highway for nearly three hours.  车流在被淹的公路上缓慢行进了近三个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hum</td><td>/ˈhʌm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嗡嗡作响；哼唱</td><td>hum a tune<br>哼一首曲子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The refrigerator hums quietly all through the night.  冰箱整夜轻轻嗡嗡作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overflow</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈfloʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>溢出，泛滥</td><td>overflow the river banks<br>河水漫过堤岸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Melting snow made the river overflow its banks last spring.  去年春天融雪使河水漫过堤岸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pullulate</del></td><td>/ˈpʌljəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>迅速繁殖，充满</td><td>pullulate with insects<br>满是昆虫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shallow pond pullulated with tadpoles after the rains.  雨后浅池塘里蝌蚪繁生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swarm</td><td>/ˈswɔːrm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>蜂拥，云集；一大群</td><td>swarm into the square<br>蜂拥进入广场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters swarmed into the square before the announcement.  记者们在宣布之前蜂拥进入广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 teem</td><td>/ˈtiːm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>充满，大量出现</td><td>teem with fish<br>鱼类繁多</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The river teems with fish every spring.  每年春天这条河里鱼类繁多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 center</td><td>/ˈsentər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使集中；中心</td><td>center attention on quality<br>把注意力集中于质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report centers its argument on three very clear examples.  报告把论点集中在三个非常清晰的例子上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 core</td><td>/ˈkɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，要点</td><td>the core of the argument<br>论点的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At the core of the dispute lies a simple money question.  争端的核心是一个简单的金钱问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heart</td><td>/ˈhɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，中心；心脏</td><td>the heart of the matter<br>问题的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Let us get to the heart of the matter quickly.  我们快点谈到问题的核心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kernel</td><td>/ˈkɜːrnəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，要点；果核</td><td>the kernel of truth<br>一点真实的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a kernel of truth in this otherwise absurd rumor.  这个荒唐的谣言里有一点真实的核心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lack</td><td>/ˈlæk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>缺乏，不足</td><td>a lack of funds<br>资金不足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project stalled because of a serious lack of funds.  项目因严重缺乏资金而停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 need</td><td>/ˈniːd/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>需要，必需</td><td>need urgent help<br>需要紧急帮助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Plants need sunlight and water to grow properly indoors.  室内植物需要阳光和水才能正常生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 want</td><td>/ˈwɑːnt/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>缺乏，短缺</td><td>for want of evidence<br>因缺乏证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project collapsed for want of steady funding and clear leadership.  项目因缺乏稳定资金和明确领导而失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bumble</b></td><td>/ˈbʌmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>含糊不清地说，杂乱无章地说</td><td>bumble through a speech<br>含糊地讲完演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bumbled through his answer and confused the whole audience.  他含糊其辞地答完，把全场听众都弄糊涂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 babble</td><td>/ˈbæbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>发出含糊无意义的嘟囔声，胡乱说</td><td>babble on about nothing<br>没完没了地瞎扯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The toddler would babble happily for hours in his crib.  这个学步的孩子会在婴儿床里开心地咿呀乱说几个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drivel</td><td>/ˈdrɪvl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胡言乱语</td><td>talk complete drivel<br>满口胡言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet talked complete drivel about curing serious illness with colored light.  那本小册子满口胡言，说用彩色光能治重病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drool</td><td>/ˈdruːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>垂涎；过分夸赞</td><td>drool over the new car<br>对新车垂涎不已</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fans drooled over every photo of the new sports car.  粉丝们对新跑车的每张照片都垂涎不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gabble</td><td>/ˈɡæbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>急促而含糊地说</td><td>gabble away nervously<br>紧张地急促乱说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child gabbled excitedly about everything he had seen that day.  孩子兴奋地急促讲述那天见到的一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gibber</td><td>/ˈdʒɪbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>语无伦次地快说</td><td>gibber with fright<br>吓得语无伦次</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was gibbering with terror and no one could understand him.  他吓得语无伦次，没人能听懂他说什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jabber</td><td>/ˈdʒæbər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>快而不清楚地说</td><td>jabber away in Italian<br>用意大利语急促地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tourists jabbered away in Italian throughout the entire bus ride.  游客们在整段车程中都用意大利语喋喋不休。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prattle</td><td>/ˈprætəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>闲扯，唠叨</td><td>prattle on for hours<br>唠叨好几个小时</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She prattled happily about her garden while we drank our tea.  我们喝茶时她开心地闲扯她的花园。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sputter</td><td>/ˈspʌtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>语无伦次地说；噗噗作响</td><td>sputter with rage<br>气得语无伦次</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sputtered with rage and could not finish the sentence.  他气得语无伦次，那句话没说完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 articulate</td><td>/ɑːrˈtɪkjuleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>清晰地表达；表达清晰的</td><td>articulate her concerns<br>清晰表达她的顾虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She managed to articulate her concerns clearly at the board meeting.  她在董事会上清晰地表达了自己的顾虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enunciate</td><td>/ɪˈnʌnsieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、及物动词</td><td>清晰地说；公开宣布，宣称</td><td>enunciate each word clearly<br>清晰吐出每个词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please enunciate each word clearly so the people at the back can hear.  请把每个词清晰地吐出，好让后排的人听清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pronounce</td><td>/prəˈnaʊns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>发音；宣布</td><td>pronounce a word clearly<br>把一个词读清楚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She pronounced each word slowly for the beginners.  她为初学者慢慢读出每个词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>bustle</b></td><td>/ˈbʌsl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>忙乱，喧闹；（快速地）行走，奔忙</td><td>the bustle of the street<br>街上的喧闹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I love the bustle of the market on Saturday mornings.  我喜欢周六早晨市场上的喧闹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluster</td><td>/ˈblʌstər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>狂妄自大地大声说；喧闹的状态</td><td>bluster and threaten<br>大声吓唬威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager began to bluster and threaten when questioned closely.  被仔细追问时，经理开始狂妄自大地大声吓唬威胁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coil</td><td>/ˈkɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>盘绕，卷起；混乱骚动</td><td>coil a rope<br>把绳子盘起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He coiled the wet rope neatly and hung it up.  他把湿绳整齐盘好挂了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturbance</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrbəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骚乱，扰乱</td><td>cause a disturbance<br>引起骚乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police were called after a disturbance broke out in the bar.  酒吧里发生骚乱后警察被叫来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 furor</td><td>/ˈfjʊrɔːr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，狂怒；激动，狂热</td><td>cause a public furor<br>引起公众哗然</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting caused a public furor when it was first shown.  这幅画首次展出时引起公众哗然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hurricane</td><td>/ˈhɜːrəkeɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>飓风般的事物，引起动荡的事物</td><td>a hurricane of protest<br>抗议的风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hurricane of protest followed the sudden announcement of the new tax.  新税突然宣布之后，随即掀起一场抗议的风暴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pandemonium</td><td>/ˌpændɪˈmoʊniəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，一片混乱</td><td>pandemonium broke out<br>顿时一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pandemonium broke out when the final goal was scored.  最后一个进球时全场一片沸腾混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stir</td><td>/ˈstɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，煽动；搅拌</td><td>stir up anger<br>激起怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The article stirred up anger among local residents.  这篇文章激起了当地居民的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 storm</td><td>/ˈstɔːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暴风雨；一阵猛烈（如抗议）</td><td>a storm of protest<br>一阵抗议风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision caused a storm of protest on social media.  这一决定在社交媒体上引发抗议风暴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumult</td><td>/ˈtuːmʌlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骚动，暴动</td><td>the tumult of the crowd<br>人群的骚动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tumult in the square grew louder as more protesters arrived.  随着更多抗议者到来，广场上的骚动越来越大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turmoil</td><td>/ˈtɜːrmɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，动荡，骚动</td><td>in political turmoil<br>处于政治动荡中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country remained in political turmoil for nearly a decade.  这个国家在近十年里一直处于政治动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproar</td><td>/ˈʌˌprɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，骚动</td><td>cause an uproar<br>引起轩然大波</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax plan caused an uproar among small business owners.  新税收方案在小企业主中引起轩然大波。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 welter</td><td>/ˈweltər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>混乱，动乱</td><td>a welter of details<br>杂乱的一堆细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report buried its main point in a welter of small details.  这份报告把要点埋在了杂乱的琐碎细节之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 whirl</td><td>/ˈwɜːrl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>急转，飞旋；旋转</td><td>whirl around<br>猛地转过身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Snowflakes whirled madly in the beam of the streetlight.  雪花在路灯光柱中疯狂飞旋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blast</td><td>/blæst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>爆炸；斥责，抨击</td><td>a bomb blast<br>炸弹爆炸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bomb blast shattered every window on the narrow street.  一次炸弹爆炸震碎了这条窄街上的每扇窗户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bolt</td><td>/ˈboʊlt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>突然说出；猛冲</td><td>bolt out an answer<br>脱口说出答案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bolted out the secret before anyone could stop him.  他还没等人阻止就把秘密说了出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careen</td><td>/kəˈriːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃疾行，横冲直撞</td><td>careen down the hill<br>摇晃着冲下山坡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empty cart careened down the hill and struck a fence.  空车摇晃着冲下山坡，撞上了篱笆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 career</td><td>/kəˈrɪr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>疾驰，飞奔</td><td>career down the hill<br>沿山坡疾驰而下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empty cart careered down the slope and hit a fence.  空车沿坡疾冲而下撞上了栅栏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dash</td><td>/ˈdæʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>猛冲，疾奔</td><td>dash across the street<br>快速冲过街道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dashed across the street to catch the departing bus.  她冲过街道去赶那辆即将开走的公交车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fly</td><td>/ˈflaɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>飞奔，疾行；飞</td><td>fly down the street<br>沿街飞奔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She flew down the corridor to catch the departing train.  她飞奔穿过走廊去赶将要开走的火车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hustle</td><td>/ˈhʌsəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>催促；奋力行进</td><td>hustle through the crowd<br>在人群中挤行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hustled the children into the car before the rain.  她在下雨前把孩子们匆匆赶上车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rush</td><td>/rʌʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>匆忙，急速行动；催促</td><td>rush to the airport<br>赶往机场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They rushed to the airport and barely caught the flight.  他们赶往机场，勉强赶上了航班。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rustle</td><td>/ˈrʌsl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>快速地行动</td><td>rustle up a meal<br>快手弄出一顿饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She rustled up a hot meal for six guests in twenty minutes.  她二十分钟内为六位客人麻利地做出一顿热饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 speed</td><td>/ˈspiːd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>快速行进，疾驰</td><td>speed down the highway<br>在公路上疾驰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ambulance sped down the empty highway toward the hospital.  救护车沿着空旷的公路疾驰驶向医院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hush</td><td>/ˈhʌʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使安静，平息</td><td>hush the crying baby<br>哄哭闹的婴儿安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hushed the restless children with one long story.  她用一个长故事把躁动的孩子们哄安静了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peace</td><td>/ˈpiːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和平，和睦；平静</td><td>live in peace<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two villages have lived in peace for over a century.  这两个村庄和睦相处已逾一个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quiet</td><td>/ˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安静的；平静的</td><td>keep the room quiet<br>保持房间安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library stays quiet even during exam week.  即使在考试周，图书馆也保持安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rest</td><td>/ˈrest/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>休息，静止；剩余部分</td><td>take a short rest<br>稍作休息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor ordered him to rest for at least a week.  医生要他至少休息一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stillness</td><td>/ˈstɪlnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>寂静，静止</td><td>the stillness of dawn<br>黎明时的寂静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a distant bell broke the stillness of the valley.  只有远处的钟声打破了山谷的寂静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tranquillity</td><td>/træŋˈkwɪlɪti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，平静</td><td>rural tranquillity<br>乡间的宁静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved to the coast for peace and tranquillity.  他们为了平静与宁静搬到了海边。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 crawl</td><td>/ˈkrɔːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢行进；爬行</td><td>crawl along the highway<br>在公路上缓慢行进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crawled along the flooded highway for nearly three hours.  车流在被淹的公路上缓慢行进了近三个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 creep</td><td>/kriːp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢地行进</td><td>creep along slowly<br>缓慢地爬行前进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crept forward only a few meters at a time all morning.  整个上午车流每次只缓慢前进几米。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lag</td><td>/læɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>落后，行动缓慢</td><td>lag behind the group<br>落在队伍后面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The youngest hiker lagged far behind the group on the steep trail.  最年幼的徒步者在陡峭的小道上远远落在队伍后面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 poke</td><td>/ˈpoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>缓慢行进，闲荡</td><td>poke along the road<br>沿路慢慢晃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old truck poked along the highway at thirty miles an hour.  那辆旧卡车以每小时三十英里在公路上慢慢晃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>calamity</b></td><td>/kəˈlæməti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>灾难，大祸；不幸事件</td><td>a national calamity<br>国家性的大灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The flood was the worst calamity the small town had faced.  这场洪水是这个小镇遭遇过的最严重灾难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apocalypse</td><td>/əˈpɑːkəˌlɪps/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大灾难；世界末日</td><td>a nuclear apocalypse<br>核末日灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel imagines an apocalypse caused by a runaway climate crisis.  这部小说设想了失控气候危机引发的末日灾难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cataclysm</td><td>/ˈkætəklɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大灾难；剧变</td><td>a social cataclysm<br>社会剧变</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The eruption was a cataclysm that reshaped the entire island.  那次喷发是一场重塑整座岛屿的大灾难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catastrophe</td><td>/kəˈtæstrəfi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大灾难；彻底的失败</td><td>an ecological catastrophe<br>生态大灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The oil spill became an ecological catastrophe for the whole coast.  漏油事件成为整个海岸的生态大灾难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debacle</td><td>/deɪˈbɑːkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>溃败</td><td>an economic debacle<br>经济上的溃败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The campaign ended in a debacle that cost the party three seats.  这场竞选以溃败收场，使该党丢掉三个席位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disaster</td><td>/dɪˈzæstər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>灾难；彻底的失败</td><td>a natural disaster<br>自然灾害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The opening night was a disaster from the very first scene.  首演之夜从第一场戏起就是一场彻底的失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tragedy</td><td>/ˈtrædʒədi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>惨事，灾难；悲剧</td><td>a terrible tragedy<br>一场可怕的惨剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mine collapse was a tragedy that the town never forgot.  矿井塌方是这座小镇永难忘却的惨剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benediction</td><td>/ˌbenəˈdɪkʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恩赐，福音；祝祷</td><td>a benediction for farmers<br>对农民的恩赐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain after the long drought was a benediction for local farmers.  久旱之后的这场雨是当地农民的恩赐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 boon</td><td>/buːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恩惠，福利</td><td>a boon to farmers<br>对农民的福音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This solar charger is a real boon to travelling photographers.  这款太阳能充电器对旅行摄影师来说是一大福音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 felicity</td><td>/fɪˈlɪsəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>得体，恰当；幸福</td><td>felicity of expression<br>措辞得体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essays are admired for their felicity of expression.  她的散文以措辞得体而受赞赏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 godsend</td><td>/ˈɡɑːdˌsend/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>天赐之物，及时的帮助</td><td>prove a godsend<br>成为天赐的帮助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The extra grant was a godsend to the small clinic.  这笔额外拨款对小诊所是天赐之助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 manna</td><td>/ˈmænə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意外的恩赐</td><td>manna from heaven<br>天降的恩赐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The donation felt like manna to the struggling school.  这笔捐款对困难的学校像是天降恩赐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 windfall</td><td>/ˈwɪndfɔːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意外之财；意外的好运</td><td>an unexpected windfall<br>意外之财</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unexpected windfall allowed them to repay the whole loan early.  这笔意外之财让他们提前还清了全部贷款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>calculated</b></td><td>/ˈkælkjuleɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过深思熟虑的，有意的</td><td>a calculated risk<br>经过权衡的风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investing now was a calculated risk that finally paid off.  现在投资是经过盘算的风险，最终有了回报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advised</td><td>/ədˈvaɪzd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过考虑的，慎重的</td><td>a well advised decision<br>深思后的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Postponing the launch was a well advised decision after all.  推迟发布终究是个经过慎重考虑的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 considered</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过深思的，慎重的</td><td>a considered opinion<br>深思后的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is my considered judgment after reading the whole report.  这是我读完整份报告后的慎重判断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deliberate</td><td>/dɪˈlɪbərət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的，故意的</td><td>a deliberate attempt<br>故意的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delay was deliberate, designed to weaken our position.  这次延误是故意的，目的是削弱我们的立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knowing</td><td>/ˈnoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>故意的，明知的</td><td>a knowing violation of the rules<br>明知故犯的违规</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge called it a knowing violation of the safety rules.  法官称这是明知故犯地违反安全规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 measured</td><td>/ˈmeʒərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的，审慎的</td><td>a measured response<br>审慎的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister gave a measured response that neither denied nor confirmed the accusations.  部长做出了审慎的回应，既未否认也未证实这些指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reasoned</td><td>/ˈriːzənd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过推理的，理由充分的</td><td>a reasoned argument<br>有理有据的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She presented a reasoned case for keeping the library open.  她提出了有理有据的理由要求图书馆继续开放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 studied</td><td>/ˈstʌdid/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的；刻意的</td><td>studied indifference<br>刻意装出的冷淡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered with studied calm that impressed the whole panel.  他以刻意保持的镇定作答，让整个评审组印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thoughtful</td><td>/ˈθɔːtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的；体贴的</td><td>a thoughtful measured reply<br>深思熟虑的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a thoughtful reply after considering the question carefully.  她仔细考虑问题后给出深思熟虑的回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weighed</td><td>/ˈweɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>斟酌过的，慎重的</td><td>carefully weighed words<br>字字斟酌的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every sentence in the letter was carefully weighed.  信中每句话都经过仔细斟酌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 automatic</td><td>/ˌɔːtəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>机械的，不经思索的；自动的</td><td>an automatic apology<br>机械的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He murmured an automatic apology and hurried past me.  他机械地含糊道了个歉，匆匆从我身边走过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 casual</td><td>/ˈkæʒəwəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>随意的，非故意的</td><td>a casual remark<br>随口的一句话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A casual remark at dinner accidentally revealed the whole secret.  晚餐时随口一句话意外揭出了整个秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 instinctive</td><td>/ɪnˈstɪŋktɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>本能的，直觉的</td><td>an instinctive reaction<br>本能的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her instinctive distrust of the offer proved entirely correct.  她对这份提议本能的不信任完全正确。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spontaneous</td><td>/spɑːnˈteɪniəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自发的，不经思索的</td><td>a spontaneous burst of applause<br>自发的一阵掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A spontaneous burst of applause followed the young pianist's final note.  年轻钢琴家弹出最后一个音，掌声自发响起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unstudied</td><td>/ʌnˈstʌdid/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自然的，不做作的</td><td>unstudied natural charm<br>天然不做作的魅力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unstudied charm won over the entire skeptical audience.  她自然不做作的魅力赢得了全场持疑观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>canny</b></td><td>/ˈkæni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>精明的，聪明的</td><td>a canny investor<br>精明的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A canny investor sold his shares just before the crash.  一位精明的投资者在崩盘前卖掉了股票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 astute</td><td>/əˈstuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>机敏的，有洞察力的</td><td>an astute observer<br>敏锐的观察者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An astute observer would have noticed the tension in the room.  一个机敏的观察者会注意到房间里的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clever</td><td>/ˈklevər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，机敏的</td><td>a clever solution<br>巧妙的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That was a clever way to avoid the extra cost.  那是避免额外成本的聪明办法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hardheaded</td><td>/ˈhɑːrdˌhedɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>固执的；务实精明的</td><td>a hardheaded negotiator<br>强硬的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her hardheaded refusal to compromise stalled the talks for weeks.  她固执地拒绝妥协，使谈判停滞了数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heady</td><td>/ˈhedi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>使人兴奋的；精明的</td><td>a heady mix of ideas<br>令人兴奋的思想混合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Success brought a heady sense of freedom.  成功带来令人兴奋的自由感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knowing</td><td>/ˈnoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>故意的，明知的</td><td>a knowing violation of the rules<br>明知故犯的违规</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge called it a knowing violation of the safety rules.  法官称这是明知故犯地违反安全规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savvy</td><td>/ˈsævi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>老练，机智；有见识的，精明能干的</td><td>political savvy<br>政治上的老练</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her political savvy helped the bill pass with almost no opposition.  她政治上的老练使那项议案几乎无异议通过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sharp</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖刻的；锋利的；敏锐的</td><td>a sharp tongue<br>说话尖刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic has a sharp tongue but an unusually fair mind.  这位评论家言辞尖刻，心思却格外公正。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrewd</td><td>/ʃruːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精明的，机敏的</td><td>a shrewd judgment<br>精明的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a shrewd judgment that saved the company millions of dollars.  那是个精明的判断，为公司省下了数百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignorant</td><td>/ˈɪɡnərənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知的，不知道的</td><td>ignorant of the facts<br>不了解事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They were completely ignorant of the danger ahead.  他们对前方的危险毫不知情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unknowing</td><td>/ʌnˈnoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不知情的，无意识的</td><td>an unknowing accomplice<br>不知情的帮凶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was an unknowing carrier of the virus for weeks.  她在数周内一直是不知情的病毒携带者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>canonize</b></td><td>/ˈkænənaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使神圣，封为圣徒</td><td>canonize a saint<br>封为圣徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The church voted to canonize the nun a century after her death.  教会在这位修女去世百年后决定将她封为圣徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggrandize</td><td>/əˈɡrændaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扩大（权力、财富、声望）</td><td>aggrandize one's power<br>扩张自己的权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor used the crisis to aggrandize his own power.  市长利用危机扩大自己的权力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deify</td><td>/ˈdeɪɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>神化，奉若神明</td><td>deify a leader<br>神化领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime tried to deify its founder in every textbook.  该政权试图在每本教科书里神化其创始人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dignify</td><td>/ˈdɪɡnəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使有尊严，使显庄重</td><td>dignify the ceremony<br>使典礼显得庄重</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her quiet presence dignified the otherwise chaotic opening ceremony.  她安静的在场使原本混乱的开幕式显得庄重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elevate</td><td>/ˈelɪveɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提升，提高；使兴奋</td><td>elevate the mind<br>提升心智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good literature can elevate the mind and refine one's taste.  优秀的文学能提升心智、陶冶品味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ennoble</td><td>/ɪˈnoʊbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尊贵，使高尚</td><td>ennoble the spirit<br>使精神高尚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Suffering, he believed, could ennoble an ordinary life.  他相信苦难能使平凡的人生变得高贵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enshrine</td><td>/enˈʃraɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>奉为神圣，珍藏</td><td>enshrine a right<br>将权利奉为神圣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The constitution enshrines the right to free speech.  宪法将言论自由的权利奉为神圣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ensky</del></td><td>/ɪnˈskaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使升天，奉上天界</td><td>ensky a hero<br>把英雄奉上天界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old poem enskies the fallen warriors as bright stars.  这首古诗把阵亡战士奉为明亮的星辰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enthrone</td><td>/ɪnˈθroʊn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使登王位，拥立</td><td>enthrone the young prince<br>拥立年轻王子登位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobles enthroned the young prince before his tenth birthday.  贵族们在小王子十岁前拥立他登位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glorify</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːrəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，美化</td><td>glorify war and violence<br>美化战争与暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics said the film glorified war instead of questioning it.  批评者说这部电影美化战争而非质疑它。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnify</td><td>/ˈmæɡnəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放大；夸大</td><td>magnify the problem<br>夸大问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lens magnifies the specimen four hundred times.  这个镜头把样本放大四百倍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abase</td><td>/əˈbeɪs/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>降低（地位、威望、尊严）</td><td>abase oneself before someone<br>在某人面前自贬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He would not abase himself by begging for the job.  他不愿低声下气地乞求这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 degrade</td><td>/dɪˈɡreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>贬低，使降级；使退化</td><td>degrade the quality<br>降低质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such insulting jokes degrade everyone who repeats them.  这类侮辱性的笑话贬低了每个转述它们的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 demean</td><td>/dɪˈmiːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低，降低尊严</td><td>demean oneself<br>自贬身份</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He refused to demean his colleagues in front of clients.  他拒绝在客户面前贬低同事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humiliate</td><td>/hjuːˈmɪlieɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>羞辱，使丧失尊严</td><td>humiliate him in public<br>当众羞辱他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher never humiliated a student for asking a simple question.  这位老师从不因学生问简单的问题而羞辱他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>captious</b></del></td><td>/ˈkæpʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吹毛求疵的，爱挑毛病的</td><td>a captious critic<br>吹毛求疵的批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His captious comments on every draft discouraged the young writers.  他对每份草稿吹毛求疵的评论让年轻作者们气馁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carping</td><td>/ˈkɑːrpɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吹毛求疵的，唠叨挑剔的</td><td>carping criticism<br>挑剔的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His carping criticism discouraged the whole team.  他挑剔的批评让整个团队泄气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 caviling</del></td><td>/ˈkævəlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无理挑剔的</td><td>caviling objections<br>无理挑剔的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board dismissed his caviling objections at once.  董事会立即驳回了他无理挑剔的反对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 critical</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>关键的；批评的</td><td>a critical moment<br>关键时刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water supply is critical to the survival of this town.  供水对这座小镇的存续至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 faultfinding</del></td><td>/ˈfɔːltˌfaɪndɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>挑剔的；找错，指摘</td><td>constant faultfinding<br>不断的挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her constant faultfinding wore out every new assistant.  她不断的挑剔让每个新助手都疲惫不堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hypercritical</td><td>/ˌhaɪpərˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分挑剔的，苛刻的</td><td>a hypercritical reader<br>过分挑剔的读者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her hypercritical remarks discouraged the whole design team.  她过分挑剔的评论让整个设计团队泄气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 Judgmental</td><td>/dʒʌdʒˈmentəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱评判的，好指责的</td><td>a judgmental tone<br>好指责的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Try not to sound so judgmental when giving feedback.  给反馈时尽量别显得那么爱评判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 overcritical</del></td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于挑剔的</td><td>an overcritical parent<br>过于挑剔的家长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An overcritical parent can easily destroy a child's confidence.  过于挑剔的家长很容易毁掉孩子的自信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appreciative</td><td>/əˈpriːʃiˌeɪtɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>赞赏的，感激的</td><td>an appreciative audience<br>懂得欣赏的观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pianist played for a warm and appreciative audience.  钢琴家为热情且懂欣赏的观众演奏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>careen</b></td><td>/kəˈriːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃疾行，横冲直撞</td><td>careen down the hill<br>摇晃着冲下山坡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empty cart careened down the hill and struck a fence.  空车摇晃着冲下山坡，撞上了篱笆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodder</td><td>/ˈdɑːdər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚，颤巍巍地行进</td><td>dodder along the path<br>沿小路蹒跚而行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man doddered along the path with a cane in each hand.  老人两手各持一杖，沿小路蹒跚而行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lurch</td><td>/lɜːrtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚</td><td>lurch to one side<br>向一侧猛地歪倒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bus lurched to one side and threw passengers against the windows.  公交车猛地向一侧歪倒，把乘客甩到窗户上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reel</td><td>/riːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>感到眩晕</td><td>reel from the blow<br>被打得头昏眼花</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boxer reeled from the blow and clutched at the ropes.  拳手被那一击打得头昏眼花，抓住了围绳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sway</td><td>/ˈsweɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>支配，统治；摇摆</td><td>hold sway over<br>支配，统治…</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For decades one family held sway over the entire province.  几十年间，一个家族统治着整个省份。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 teeter</td><td>/ˈtiːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，摇摇欲坠</td><td>teeter on the edge<br>在边缘摇摇欲坠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company teetered on the edge of bankruptcy for several months.  公司数月来一直在破产边缘摇摇欲坠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 totter</td><td>/ˈtɑːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇摇晃晃地走，蹒跚</td><td>totter across the room<br>摇晃着走过房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The toddler tottered a few steps and then sat down.  幼儿摇摇晃晃走了几步就坐下了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waddle</td><td>/ˈwɑːdl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇摇摆摆地走</td><td>waddle like a duck<br>像鸭子般摇摆走</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fat puppy waddled across the kitchen toward its water bowl.  那只胖胖的小狗摇摇摆摆地穿过厨房走向水碗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wobble</td><td>/ˈwɑːbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，颤抖；犹豫不决</td><td>wobble on the ice<br>在冰上摇晃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old table wobbles whenever someone leans on its left side.  只要有人靠在左边，这张旧桌子就会摇晃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>carefree</b></td><td>/ˈkerfriː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无忧无虑的</td><td>a carefree summer<br>无忧无虑的夏天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a carefree summer swimming in the lake every day.  我们度过了一个无忧无虑的夏天，每天在湖里游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blithe</td><td>/blaɪð/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快高兴的；无忧无虑的；漫不经心的</td><td>a blithe disregard<br>漫不经心的无视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His blithe disregard for the rules annoyed the older staff.  他对规则漫不经心的无视让资深员工不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debonair</td><td>/ˌdebəˈner/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>风度翩翩的，温文尔雅的</td><td>a debonair manner<br>从容优雅的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debonair gentleman charmed every woman in the crowded ballroom.  这位风度翩翩的绅士让拥挤舞厅里的每位女士都为之倾倒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gay</td><td>/ˈɡeɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，欢快的</td><td>gay laughter<br>欢快的笑声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Gay music and bright lanterns filled the village square that evening.  那晚欢快的音乐和明亮的灯笼充满了村中广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insouciant</td><td>/ɪnˈsuːsiənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无忧虑的，不在乎的</td><td>an insouciant shrug<br>满不在乎地耸肩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the harsh criticism with an insouciant shrug and a smile.  他对严厉的批评只是满不在乎地耸耸肩，笑了笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lighthearted</td><td>/ˌlaɪtˈhɑːrtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻松愉快的，无忧无虑的</td><td>a lighthearted comedy<br>轻松的喜剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We wanted a lighthearted film after such a stressful week.  在如此紧张的一周后，我们想看部轻松的电影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 lightsome</del></td><td>/ˈlaɪtsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻快的，快活的</td><td>a lightsome step<br>轻快的步子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She crossed the room with a lightsome, dancing step.  她以轻快如舞的步子穿过房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 slaphappy</del></td><td>/ˈslæpˌhæpi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻率乐观的，晕头转向的</td><td>a slaphappy attitude<br>轻率乐观的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their slaphappy attitude to safety worried the inspector.  他们对安全轻率的态度让检查员担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unconcerned</td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈsɜːrnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不关心的，漠不关心的</td><td>unconcerned about risk<br>对风险漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seemed entirely unconcerned about the noise from the street.  她似乎完全不在意街上的噪音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careworn</td><td>/ˈkerwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>憔悴的，饱受忧虑的</td><td>a careworn face<br>憔悴的面容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her careworn face brightened when the children ran into the room.  孩子们跑进房间时，她憔悴的脸上亮了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cascade</b></td><td>/kæsˈkeɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>一连串，大量</td><td>a cascade of complaints<br>一连串投诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cascade of complaints followed the sudden increase in fares.  票价突然上涨后，投诉接连不断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deluge</td><td>/ˈdeljuːdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大暴雨；大量</td><td>a deluge of complaints<br>大量投诉涌来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The announcement brought a deluge of angry letters to the editor.  这项公告引来大量愤怒的读者来信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avalanche</td><td>/ˈævəˌlæntʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>雪崩；大量涌来</td><td>an avalanche of letters<br>如雪崩般的信件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The announcement triggered an avalanche of angry emails.  这则公告引发了如雪崩般的愤怒邮件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cellular</b></td><td>/ˈseljələr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多孔的；细胞的</td><td>a cellular structure<br>多孔的结构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plant stem has a light cellular structure inside.  这种植物的茎内部是轻质的多孔结构。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 porous</td><td>/ˈpɔːrəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多孔可渗透的</td><td>porous volcanic rock<br>多孔的火山岩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water seeps very quickly through the porous volcanic rock here.  水很快渗过这里多孔的火山岩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ceremonious</b></td><td>/ˌserəˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>讲究礼节的，庄重的</td><td>a ceremonious bow<br>郑重的鞠躬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ambassador greeted the guests with a ceremonious bow.  大使以郑重的鞠躬迎接客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 correct</td><td>/kəˈrekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>得体的，合礼的；正确的</td><td>correct behavior<br>得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His correct behavior impressed the elderly hosts.  他得体的举止令年长的主人印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decorous</td><td>/ˈdekərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体端正的</td><td>decorous behavior at dinner<br>晚餐时得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guests kept a decorous silence while the old bishop said grace.  老主教做餐前祷告时，客人们保持着得体的安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 formal</td><td>/ˈfɔːrməl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正式的，规范的</td><td>formal written English<br>正式书面英语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Use formal language when writing to the committee.  给委员会写信时要用正式语言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nice</td><td>/ˈnaɪs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>规矩得体的；令人愉快的</td><td>nice manners<br>得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a nice boy who never gives his parents trouble.  他是个规矩的男孩，从不给父母添麻烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proper</td><td>/ˈprɑːpər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适当的，正确的</td><td>the proper procedure<br>正确的程序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please store the samples in the proper containers.  请把样本存放在合适的容器里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 punctilious</td><td>/pʌŋkˈtɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>注意细节的，一丝不苟的</td><td>punctilious about details<br>对细节一丝不苟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is punctilious about details that others simply ignore.  他对别人干脆忽略的细节一丝不苟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 starchy</td><td>/ˈstɑːrtʃi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拘谨古板的；含淀粉的</td><td>a starchy manner<br>拘谨古板的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new director was starchy and never joined the lunch table.  新主管很古板，从不来午餐桌上坐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stiff</td><td>/stɪf/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>僵硬的，无法弯曲的；艰苦的，费力的</td><td>a stiff leather collar<br>硬挺的皮质衣领</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stiff leather collar rubbed his neck raw during the long march.  长途行军中，硬挺的皮领把他的脖子磨破了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stilted</td><td>/ˈstɪltɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>生硬的，不自然的</td><td>stilted conversation<br>生硬的交谈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their stilted conversation died out before the soup arrived.  他们生硬的交谈在汤上来前就断了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 casual</td><td>/ˈkæʒəwəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>随意的，非故意的</td><td>a casual remark<br>随口的一句话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A casual remark at dinner accidentally revealed the whole secret.  晚餐时随口一句话意外揭出了整个秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 easygoing</td><td>/ˌiːziˈɡoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>随和的，脾气好的</td><td>an easygoing manner<br>随和的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her easygoing nature makes her popular with colleagues.  她随和的性格使她受同事欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 informal</td><td>/ɪnˈfɔːrməl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正式的，随意的</td><td>an informal meeting<br>非正式会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They settled the dispute through informal talks over coffee.  他们在咖啡桌旁通过非正式交谈解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unceremonious</td><td>/ˌʌnserəˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>唐突无礼的，草率的</td><td>an unceremonious dismissal<br>唐突的解职</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unceremonious exit surprised the guests still waiting for dessert.  他唐突离场，让还等着甜点的客人吃了一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>certitude</b></td><td>/ˈsɜːrtɪtuːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确信无疑</td><td>moral certitude<br>道德上的确信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spoke of the plan's success with unshakable certitude.  他以不可动摇的确信谈起这项计划的成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assurance</td><td>/əˈʃʊrəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>保证；自信，确信</td><td>give an assurance<br>作出保证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered the difficult questions with surprising assurance.  她以惊人的自信回答了那些难题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assuredness</td><td>/əˈʃʊrdnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确信，笃定自信</td><td>quiet assuredness<br>沉静的笃定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke with a quiet assuredness that convinced us all.  她以沉静的笃定说话，说服了我们所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 certainty</td><td>/ˈsɜːrtənti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确定，必然；确信</td><td>with absolute certainty<br>绝对确定地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one can predict the outcome with absolute certainty.  没人能绝对确定地预测结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confidence</td><td>/ˈkɑːnfədəns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>信心，确信</td><td>full of confidence<br>充满信心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered every question with quiet confidence.  他从容而自信地回答了每个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conviction</td><td>/kənˈvɪkʃn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>深信，确信；证明有罪</td><td>speak with conviction<br>满怀信念地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She argued her case with a conviction that silenced every critic.  她以坚定的信念陈述自己的主张，令所有批评者沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 doubtlessness</del></td><td>/ˈdaʊtləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确定无疑，毫无怀疑</td><td>complete doubtlessness<br>完全的确信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He asserted his innocence with complete doubtlessness.  他以完全确信的态度声称自己无罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 positiveness</del></td><td>/ˈpɑːzɪtɪvnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确信，肯定</td><td>absolute positiveness<br>绝对的确信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her positiveness about the date turned out to be mistaken.  她对日期的确信最终证明是错的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sureness</td><td>/ˈʃʊrnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确信；稳准</td><td>the sureness of his touch<br>他手法的稳准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She played the difficult passage with remarkable sureness.  她极其稳准地演奏了那个难段。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 surety</td><td>/ˈʃʊrəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>担保人；确信</td><td>stand surety for someone<br>为某人担保</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncle agreed to act as surety for the loan.  他叔叔同意为这笔贷款作担保。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doubt</td><td>/ˈdaʊt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>怀疑，疑虑</td><td>cast doubt on<br>使人怀疑…</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is little doubt that the data was carelessly recorded.  几乎可以肯定，这些数据记录得很草率。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 dubiety</del></td><td>/duˈbaɪəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>怀疑，疑惑</td><td>a note of dubiety<br>一丝怀疑的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A trace of dubiety showed in the professor's careful reply.  教授谨慎的回答中透出一丝怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 incertitude</del></td><td>/ɪnˈsɜːrtɪtuːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不确定，疑虑</td><td>a state of incertitude<br>疑虑不定的状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months of incertitude about funding paralyzed the whole project.  数月对资金的不确定使整个项目停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncertainty</td><td>/ʌnˈsɜːrtənti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>不确定，疑惑</td><td>a period of uncertainty<br>不确定的时期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors hate a long period of political uncertainty.  投资者厌恶长期的政治不确定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>channel</b></td><td>/ˈtʃænl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引导；把…导入</td><td>channel funds into research<br>把资金投入研究</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government will channel more funds into basic medical research.  政府将把更多资金投入基础医学研究。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 canalize</del></td><td>/ˈkænəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引导，使循渠道流动</td><td>canalize public anger<br>引导公众的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The leader tried to canalize public anger into peaceful reform.  领导者试图把公众怒气引向和平改革。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 channelize</del></td><td>/ˈtʃænəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引导，导入渠道</td><td>channelize resources<br>引导资源投向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The agency channelized resources toward rural clinics.  该机构把资源引向乡村诊所。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conduct</td><td>/kənˈdʌkt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>引导，带领；实施</td><td>conduct visitors around<br>带领参观者四处走</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A young monk conducted the visitors around the ancient temple.  一位年轻僧人带领参观者游览这座古寺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 direct</td><td>/dəˈrekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直接的，径直的</td><td>a direct flight<br>直飞航班</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ask for a direct answer instead of a long explanation.  要个直接的答复，而不是长篇解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 funnel</td><td>/ˈfʌnəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>汇集导入；漏斗</td><td>funnel money into<br>把钱汇入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Donors funneled money into the new scholarship fund.  捐赠者把钱汇入新的奖学金基金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pipe</td><td>/ˈpaɪp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>用管道输送；管子</td><td>pipe water to the village<br>把水引到村里</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers piped clean water to the remote mountain village.  工程师把清水引到偏远的山村。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 siphon</td><td>/ˈsaɪfən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>抽取，虹吸</td><td>siphon off funds<br>抽走资金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager siphoned off funds from the charity account.  经理从慈善账户中抽走资金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>chaperone</b></td><td>/ˈʃæpəroʊn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>同行，护送</td><td>chaperone the students<br>陪同学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two parents chaperoned the students on the museum trip.  两位家长陪同学生去博物馆参观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accompany</td><td>/əˈkʌmpəni/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>陪同，伴随</td><td>accompany her home<br>陪她回家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered to accompany her home after the concert.  音乐会结束后他提出陪她回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attend</td><td>/əˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>倾听，注意</td><td>attend to his words<br>留意他的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please attend carefully to what the doctor tells you.  请仔细听医生对你说的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 companion</td><td>/kəmˈpænjən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>同伴，陪伴者</td><td>a travelling companion<br>旅伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found a cheerful travelling companion on the night train.  她在夜车上遇到一位快活的旅伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 company</td><td>/ˈkʌmpəni/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陪伴，同伴；公司</td><td>keep bad company<br>结交坏朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His parents worried that he was keeping bad company at school.  他父母担心他在学校结交坏朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convoy</td><td>/ˈkɑːnˌvɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>护送，护航；车队</td><td>convoy the supplies<br>护送物资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warships convoyed the supplies across the open sea.  军舰护送物资横渡公海。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 escort</td><td>/ɪˈskɔːrt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>同行，护送</td><td>escort the guests out<br>送客人出去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two guards escorted the visiting minister to the waiting car.  两名警卫护送来访的部长上了等候的汽车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squire</td><td>/ˈskwaɪər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>陪伴（女士）；乡绅</td><td>squire a lady to dinner<br>陪女士赴宴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He squired his elderly aunt to the village dance.  他陪年长的姑妈去村里的舞会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abandon</td><td>/əˈbændən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>放纵，尽情；放弃</td><td>with reckless abandon<br>肆意放纵地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She danced with wild abandon at the wedding reception.  她在婚宴上尽情忘我地跳舞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 desert</td><td>/dɪˈzɜːrt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抛弃，遗弃</td><td>desert one's post<br>擅离职守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain refused to desert his crew in the storm.  船长拒绝在风暴中抛下船员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ditch</td><td>/ˈdɪtʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>抛弃，摆脱；沟渠</td><td>ditch the old plan<br>放弃旧计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team ditched the old design after two failed tests.  两次测试失败后，团队放弃了旧设计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dump</td><td>/dʌmp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>丢弃，倾倒；抛弃</td><td>dump the waste<br>倾倒废弃物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Factories once dumped chemical waste straight into the river.  工厂曾把化学废料直接倾倒进河里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 forsake</td><td>/fərˈseɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底放弃，抛弃</td><td>forsake one's friends<br>抛弃自己的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He would never forsake the people who raised him.  他绝不会抛弃养育他的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>charisma</b></td><td>/kəˈrɪzmə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>魅力，吸引力</td><td>personal charisma<br>个人魅力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach has enough charisma to inspire even a losing team.  这位教练的魅力足以激励一支落后的球队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 allure</td><td>/əˈlʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吸引，诱惑</td><td>the allure of fame<br>名声的诱惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap rents allure young artists to this run-down district.  低廉的租金吸引年轻艺术家来到这个破旧街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appeal</td><td>/əˈpiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>上诉；恳求，呼吁</td><td>file an appeal<br>提出上诉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lawyers will file an appeal against the verdict next week.  她的律师下周将针对判决提出上诉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attractiveness</td><td>/əˈtræktɪvnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>吸引力，魅力</td><td>the attractiveness of the offer<br>该提议的吸引力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The attractiveness of the offer surprised even her rivals.  该提议的吸引力连她的对手都感到惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 captivation</del></td><td>/ˌkæptɪˈveɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>迷人的魅力，倾心</td><td>total captivation<br>完全的倾心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The audience listened in total captivation to her voice.  观众完全被她的歌声迷住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchantment</td><td>/enˈtʃæntmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>魔法，咒语；魅力</td><td>break the enchantment<br>解除魔法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only true courage could break the enchantment on the castle.  唯有真正的勇气才能解除城堡上的魔法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fascination</td><td>/ˌfæsəˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>魅力；迷恋</td><td>a lifelong fascination<br>终生的迷恋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He had a lifelong fascination with old maps and charts.  他终生迷恋古老的地图和海图。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glamor</td><td>/ˈɡlæmər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>魅力，诱惑力</td><td>the glamor of the stage<br>舞台的魅力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The glamor of the stage drew her away from teaching.  舞台的魅力让她离开了教书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seductiveness</td><td>/sɪˈdʌktɪvnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>诱惑力，妩媚</td><td>the seductiveness of easy money<br>快钱的诱惑力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The seductiveness of easy money ruined several promising careers.  快钱的诱惑力毁了好几个有前途的事业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 witchery</td><td>/ˈwɪtʃəri/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>魅力，迷人之处；巫术</td><td>a strange witchery<br>奇异的魅力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old song held a strange witchery for him.  那首老歌对他有种奇异的魅力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repulsion</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感；排斥</td><td>a feeling of repulsion<br>一种厌恶感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photographs produced repulsion rather than sympathy.  这些照片引起的是厌恶而非同情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 repulsiveness</del></td><td>/rɪˈpʌlsɪvnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>令人厌恶，可憎</td><td>sheer repulsiveness<br>十足的令人厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell's repulsiveness drove us straight out of the kitchen.  那气味的令人厌恶把我们直接赶出厨房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>cherished</b></td><td>/ˈtʃerɪʃt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>受喜爱的，珍爱的</td><td>a cherished memory<br>珍爱的回忆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph is her most cherished memory of that summer.  这张照片是她对那个夏天最珍爱的回忆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beloved</td><td>/bɪˈlʌvd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深受喜爱的，心爱的</td><td>a beloved teacher<br>深受爱戴的老师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The beloved teacher retired after forty years at the school.  这位深受爱戴的老师在校四十年后退休。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dear</td><td>/ˈdɪr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>亲爱的，珍贵的；昂贵的</td><td>a dear old friend<br>亲爱的老朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote a long letter to a dear old friend.  她给一位亲爱的老朋友写了封长信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 favored</td><td>/ˈfeɪvərd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>受宠的，被偏爱的</td><td>the favored candidate<br>受青睐的候选人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was clearly the favored child in that large family.  她显然是那个大家庭里最受宠的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 favorite</td><td>/ˈfeɪvərɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>最喜爱的；最爱之物</td><td>my favorite movie<br>我最喜欢的电影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This old jazz record is still my favorite after twenty years.  这张老爵士唱片二十年后仍是我最喜欢的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fond</td><td>/ˈfɑːnd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>喜爱的，钟爱的</td><td>fond of music<br>喜爱音乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My grandmother was very fond of the stray cat next door.  我祖母非常喜爱隔壁那只流浪猫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loved</td><td>/ˈlʌvd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被爱的，深受喜爱的</td><td>a much loved teacher<br>深受爱戴的老师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grew up feeling loved and supported by his whole family.  他在全家的关爱与支持中长大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pet</td><td>/ˈpet/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>抚摸，宠爱；宠物</td><td>pet the cat gently<br>轻轻抚摸猫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors are allowed to pet the rabbits at the farm.  参观者可以在农场抚摸兔子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 precious</td><td>/ˈpreʃəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>珍贵的，贵重的</td><td>precious stones<br>宝石，贵重石料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crown was set with precious stones from three continents.  王冠上镶着来自三大洲的宝石。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 special</td><td>/ˈspeʃəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特别的，特殊的</td><td>a special occasion<br>特殊场合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She saved that silk dress for special occasions only.  她把那条丝质连衣裙只留到特殊场合穿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sweet</td><td>/ˈswiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>甜的；温和的，亲切的</td><td>in a sweet tone<br>以温和的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a sweet voice that calmed the frightened child.  她用温柔的声音说话，安抚了害怕的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abhorred</td><td>/əbˈhɔːrd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被憎恶的，遭痛恨的</td><td>an abhorred practice<br>令人憎恶的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Slavery was an abhorred institution that took centuries to abolish.  奴隶制是一种遭人痛恨的制度，废除它花了几个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 abominated</del></td><td>/əˈbɑːmɪneɪtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>被痛恨的，遭憎恶的</td><td>a widely abominated regime<br>广受痛恨的政权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dictator was abominated by nearly everyone in the country.  这位独裁者几乎被全国所有人痛恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 despised</td><td>/dɪˈspaɪzd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被鄙视的，遭蔑视的</td><td>a despised minority<br>受轻蔑的少数群体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax collector was the most despised man in the village.  税吏是村里最遭人鄙视的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detested</td><td>/dɪˈtestɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>被厌恶的，令人讨厌的</td><td>a detested rival<br>令人厌恶的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Broccoli remains the most detested vegetable among the children here.  西兰花仍是这里孩子们最讨厌的蔬菜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disdained</td><td>/dɪsˈdeɪnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被鄙弃的，遭轻视的</td><td>a disdained profession<br>被轻视的职业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Manual labor was long disdained by the educated elite.  体力劳动长期被知识精英所鄙视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
